Westphalia → Kassel · homophonic two-digit cipher with digraph signs and a small code · January–March 1637

Otto von der Malsburg’s cipher letters to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 1637

Go to the full text ↓

Two keys recovered from ciphertext: the numerical cipher in ten letters, and a repeating alphabetic cipher in the January postscript, whose command dispute now reads with several uncertainties.

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 28 September 2026 · updated 7 November 2026

Method: key recovered from ciphertext-only · Extent: partial (53% of the cipher) · On the map

With Larry Beck (solution)

ciphertext-only
On this pageThe letters and the keyThe deciphermentMethodThe alphabetic block of 7/17 JanuaryWhat remains uncertainFull textsSources

At a glanceCiphertext-only break · partial (53% of the cipher)

The letters
Otto von der Malsburg, general war commissary, to Landgrave Wilhelm V of Hesse-Kassel, mostly from Wesel, 15 January to 28 March 1637. With them are a French letter signed “232” and German news extracts from London in the same key.
Where
Hessisches Staatsarchiv Marburg, HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, ff. 3–33, “Korrespondenz in Chiffren mit dem Generalkommissar Otto v.d. Malsburg”. HCPortal records 496–509 mark all ten as not solved.
The ciphers
The letters use two-digit homophones, signs for common letter pairs and name codes. The ten-line alphabetic block of 7/17 January uses repeating shifts 4, 5, 3, 6, 2 over a 24-letter alphabet.
How they were deciphered
The numerical key came from annealing three letters; 9,338 of 9,736 numerical-cipher groups fall in words (95.9%), but read as sentences, after the re-readings of 22–23 October 2026, four letters build coherent sentences (ff. 3, 12–13, 25 and 28–29), though measured word by word on 24 October 2026 they read at 89.1%, 91.3%, 93.4% and 92.5%, under the 95% a complete reading needs; ff. 14, 15–17, 18, 23–24, the duplicate 30–31 and 32–33 are decoded, not read. The alphabetic key was recovered separately, then applied to a revised image transcription. Its reading builds coherent sentences and reads at 91.0% of its words, also under 95%.
What it says
The letters concern provisioning, peace talks and military cooperation. The alphabetic block discusses an unnamed man's possible departure, difficulties over command with Swedish generals and the authority he seeks.
Still open
About forty numerical code groups; the alphabetic block's 43, 45 and angular sign labelled LL; several source readings and two malformed words. No person has been identified from the alphabetic codes.
Credit
Daniel Bourdeau: numerical cipher, initial alphabetic transcription and source presentation. Larry Beck, with ChatGPT: alphabetic key recovery, critical reading and documentary investigation. Separate AI image-reading passes are not review by a human paleographer.

01 The letters and the key

Otto von der Malsburg served Landgrave Wilhelm V as general war commissary for the Hessian army in Westphalia. In 1637 Hesse-Kassel was under imperial ban, Upper Hesse was occupied, and the landgrave had moved his army into East Frisia, where he died in September. (This background is not from the sources and was not checked here.) File 4 h Nr. 1411 at Marburg keeps Malsburg’s reports from January to March of that year. Each is a clear letter with long cipher runs, and several are nearly all cipher.

folioHCPortaldatecipher groupsread

14502Jan 163769592.8%
15–16503Jan 1637 (“Triplicat” and a letter)1,08695.0%

255065 Mar / 23 Feb 1637, signed59297.5%
28–2950728 Mar 1637 (old style), signed3,16596.0%
30–31508“Duplicat” of ff. 28–29(3,241)used as a check copy
32–33509French letter signed “232”, 3 Apr 1637; news extracts of March37788.1%

The cipher is a homophonic substitution into German. The table has 89 two-digit groups: e has seven (16, 33, 48, 49, 61, 79, 84), u seven, a and o six, n, i and t five, and the rarer letters two or three. The order of the table is irregular. Common letter pairs have capital signs: N and D for ch, Y and G for ei, Z and O for st, W and P for ss, L and A for tt, R, C, E and M for mm, X, j and J for sch, H for ll, S and F for au, T and B for ff, Q for ai. Single figures are ordinary numerals (“5 [tausend] Reichsthaler”). About forty-five three-digit groups between 115 and 273 are codes for places, persons and military words. The writer leaves no word spaces and the groups are separated by dots.

The same layout, a two-digit table with digraph signs and codes from 111, appears in the key that Wilhelm Burchard Sixtinus took to Hildesheim, Bremen and Hamburg in December 1635 (HCPortal key 5). Some code numbers even coincide (193, 201, 210, 212). But Sixtinus’s table does not decipher Malsburg’s letters: the chancery issued each correspondent his own table in a common pattern. (This is an inference, not from the sources, and was not checked here.)

02 The decipherment

The quotations are from the readings of 22–23 October 2026 (HCP496_reading.md and the others, with English), in the writer’s spelling as the figures give it (a normalisation of the 22 October pass was undone on the 23rd). ⟨⟩ encloses clear text, a bracketed number is an open three-digit code, <> an open sign, [?word] a doubtful word, […] unread.

5/15 January 1637 (f. 3)

⟨… gemachet,⟩ das ich auch noch nit weis, wo hervor unsre Garnisonen hirnechst der Unterhalt her, so in Frucht ⟨und⟩ [201] bestehet, genommen werden soll; ⟨das⟩ die Contributiones vom platten Lande sowol alhir als in [239] ein Ende haben. … Es ist noch Zeit, das man sich beim König von Ungern angebe wegen der Fridenstractaten ⟨und⟩ salvum conductum vor <H#> Gesanten nach {Cöln} sollicitire.

He does not know where the garrisons’ keep is to come from now that the contributions from the open country have ended, both here and in <239>. The rations are now reckoned monthly in Kassel Viertel of grain, because the non-commissioned officers draw double bread. It is still time to press the King of Hungary (Ferdinand III) for the peace talks and for safe-conducts for the Hessian envoys to Cologne.

Wesel, 7/17 January 1637 (f. 12)

… dan durch Friesslant mitt Wagen ⟨und⟩ Pferden zu gehen ist unmoglich. … Es ist von guter Hant avisiret, dass man zu Regenspurg geschlossen, <H#> Gesanten nach {Cöln} keinen salvum conductum zu erteilen. [?Hiergegen] berichtet mich der Statthalter Nesselrot, dass der Bischoff von Wirzburg an ihn im Augustto geschriben … Mitt der [140] wurde man durch [137] apart zu {Hamburg} oder Lubeck tracti[r]en lassen.

Word has come from a good source that Regensburg (the electoral diet that made Ferdinand III King of the Romans) has resolved to give the Hessian envoys no safe-conduct to Cologne. Statthalter Nesselrode reports a letter from the Bishop of Würzburg; the alternative is separate talks at Hamburg or Lübeck.

January 1637 (ff. 14–16; decoded, not read)

The provisioning of Hermannstein (Ehrenbreitstein) and its garrison; Obrist Hoffman’s troops and routes by Marburg, Corbach and Brilon; Jan de Werth in person at Cologne (in clear). On ff. 15–16: no audience yet; notice that the truce (“Stilstant”) is ended; companies of foot and of dragoons, recruiting patents already issued; the ambassador of the States General; “er redet aber nirgent von fried machen, sagt er wolte einen finger aus der faust drumb geben”.

14/24 February 1637 (f. 18; decoded, not read)

Re-read on 23 October 2026, the sentences build German, but open three-digit codes fall in most of them. A passage that holds:

es seint aber die [212] ein Teils so erfroren, das auch etliche zu Kroppeln worden, ⟨und⟩ etliche Glidder haben mussen ablosen lassen; so seint auch viel ⟨ffreundt?⟩ hernach gestorben

The winter march: men frozen and crippled, limbs amputated, many dead since.

7/17 February 1637 (ff. 23–24; decoded, not read)

Re-read on 23 October 2026; as in f. 18, open codes break most sentences. A passage that holds:

welche er aber nunmer aus dem Fundament, das Hermanstein nit effectivement proviandiret sei, disputiret ⟨und⟩ nichts [?gestehen] wil. … Stecken also [?in der] Bruhe, wissen nit, womit wir ihn zwingen wollen, ⟨und⟩ befinden nun in [d]er That, das wir mit einem Frantzosen gehan[d]elt haben.

A dispute over whether Hermannstein had been “effectively provisioned”, and over payments and gifts to the commanders; “we find in fact that we have been dealing with a Frenchman” (Ehrenbreitstein was then held by a French garrison). Reichsthaler owed on account, ransom for prisoners, the peace talks said to be moving to Picardy, Margrave Sigismund of Brandenburg’s commission, a separate peace with the imperial commissioners, and garrisons so run down that men must leave.

5 March / 23 February 1637 (f. 25)

<120> has resolved to content the cavalry; payment for ransoms and other costs; bills of exchange still not received at Cologne.

28 March 1637, old style (ff. 28–29)

The longest letter: his journey held up for days on the Watten; twelve-pound guns, constables and ammunition; Jan de Werth between the Ruhr and <268>; whether the States General will take Hesse into their alliance and give a muster place in the Cleves country; the planned junction at Cloppenburg, Quakenbrück, Minden, Vechta, Wildeshausen and Diepholz, and the colonels Uffeln and Carpf.

The French letter and the London extracts (ff. 32–33; decoded, not read)

A letter in French signed with the number 232, 3 April 1637, whose cipher runs are French in the same key (“par la lettre de n[ost]re ambassadeur extraordinaire … à Londres”, “ledict Sengell”), and German extracts of a letter from London of 16/26 March about the Palatine cause and Prince Rupert. Large paired capitals (AA, GG, OO, XX, CC, KK) stand for names.

03 Method

The catalogue entry asked first whether Malsburg’s key was among the Hesse-Kassel key sheets of 1635–52. HCPortal describes 176 key records from the Marburg key volumes, each with the people who used it; all were fetched. None names Malsburg as a user (one list of name codes gives “Otto v. der Malßburg – 523”), and the tables of the likeliest sheets (Sixtinus 1635, a sheet of “1637 or before”, and two others) do not decipher f. 3.

The eighteen scans were transcribed group by group. Annealing a homophonic key on f. 3 alone (455 groups, about 80 symbols) gave only letter noise. Three letters together (1,575 groups in the first transcription; ff. 3, 12 and 18 now count 455 + 712 + 639 = 1,806) gave the same key from every start and German text. The capital signs were then valued where the text demanded them: no[N] = noch, la[W]en = lassen, ge[X]lo[W]en = geschlossen, Ste[A]in = Stettin. The digit table was re-annealed over all the letters with the signs fixed.

The reading was measured by splitting each deciphered run into words of 17th-century German (the Deutsches Textarchiv prints of 1470–1670, plus names and spellings checked in context). A group counts as read when all its letters fall inside words. The ten letters give 9,338 of 9,736 groups (95.9%); the 193 open code groups account for half the rest, and 97.9% of the non-code text reads. The same measure on shuffled keys gives 50–58%. It counts groups inside words, not sentences: read as sentences (reading audit, 22 October 2026), ff. 3, 14, 15–17, 28–29 (and the duplicate 30–31) and 32–33 had open codes, stray letters or noise in most sentences. On 22–23 October every letter was built into sentences on the scans, letter by letter with the decode: ff. 3, 12–13, 25 and 28–29 now hold, while ff. 18 and 23–24 turned out to have open codes in most sentences, and ff. 14, 15–17, 30–31 and 32–33 stay decoded, not read (in the German extracts of f. 33 about thirty words of a fast second hand are also unread). The letters that read, with the alphabetic block, hold about 53% of the cipher (measured: 5,434 of 10,246 tokens); each of them builds coherent sentences but reads at 89–93% of its words (measured 24 October 2026), under the 95% a complete reading needs, so even these are partial. Every group that did not read was checked again on the scans: 24 misreadings and 2 doubled groups were corrected, and 210 were confirmed as the writer wrote them.

04 The alphabetic block of 7/17 January

The alphabetic key is recovered; the reading remains critical. Larry Beck and ChatGPT continued from Bourdeau’s transcription and recovered a period-five shift cipher. The original normalized transcription has 523 alphabetic positions; a selected reading from the authentic 2834 × 4284-pixel source image has 510. Both inputs and both literal outputs are preserved. The earlier figure of 531 also counted the four characters of the two LL labels and the four letters of the blot annotation; these are not ordinary cipher letters. This contribution concerns the separate ten-line block, not the surrounding numerical cipher.

The mechanism and its checks

Index abcdefghiklmnopqrstuwxyz from 0 to 23. To decrypt, subtract repeating shifts 4,5,3,6,2 modulo 24. The first ordinary letter uses shift 4, and phase continues across all ten lines. Normalization maps ü to u, ÿ to y, and the provisional j reading to i. Treat 43, 45, the angular LL pair, punctuation and the blot marker outside the ordinary alphabetic stream.

The zero-advancement convention for 43, 45 and LL was preferred among 125 consistent modulo-five treatments. It does not identify a group, establish its expansion length or prove the amount of text beneath the blot. Re-encryption checks arithmetic and transcription consistency, not the truth of the reading.

The earlier evidence package records an exhaustive check of all 7,962,624 five-position keys against the unchanged first 112 letters with a frozen German four-letter model; the same key and top ten results were reproduced. The public verifier below is a separate, smaller replay of the published inputs, alignment and alternatives. It does not repeat that exhaustive language-model search or independently authenticate manuscript readings.

German working reading

Word division, capitalization and punctuation are editorial. Square brackets preserve an unresolved code, gap or alternative; daggers mark an unresolved literal form. The colon after schreiben is an interpretation of the sentence’s scope, not deciphered punctuation.

Der [43] stehet itzo aufm Sprunge. Wollen [45] seiner gern los sein und ihn entbehren konnen, so ist es nun Zeit, das [LL] schreiben: weil sie sehen, das er mit den Schwedischen Generalen des Commendo halber sich nicht uergleichen †wurden†, [45] Estat aber erforderte, dat sie ihre †tqouppen† [Trouppen?] bey die Schweden und die Schweden wider bey sie stossen musten, auch [LL] actiones ihme nit mer gefielen, und uber das ihme solche absolute Gewalt, wie er begerte, nit geben konten, so stelleten sie es dahin, wa[BLOT]s ihme zu thun [gefellig/gefellng]; wolten ihn auch wider seine Gelegenheit nit aufhalten, sondern ihn nach [ihrem/khrem] †itzigdn† [itzigen?] Estat und Moglichkeit contentire[n?] lassen.

English, retaining the possible proposed-letter scope

[43] is now on the point of making a move [perhaps departing]. If [45] wish to be rid of him and can do without him, now is the time for [LL] to write [perhaps to the following effect]: since they see that he would not reach agreement with the Swedish generals over command [the German has singular er with plural wurden], while the situation of [45] required the joining of their [troops?] to the Swedes and, reciprocally, of the Swedes to them; and the actions associated with [LL] no longer pleased him; and moreover [they] could not give him the absolute authority he demanded, they would leave the matter to what [blot in the German] he found [pleasing?] to do. They would also not detain him against his convenience or circumstances, but would have him given satisfaction according to [their?] [present?] circumstances and ability [the final n in contentiren is source-uncertain].

The opening is conditional: it does not prove that anyone wanted him dismissed. The clauses after schreiben may give proposed letter contents; alternatively, a recommendation may be followed by reported circumstances. Neither version warrants turning non-detention and satisfaction into completed decisions. Reciprocal joining of forces is necessary in the passage, not reported as accomplished. French service, a particular employer, payment, arrears and formal discharge are not established here. Different code groups need not denote different people, and a group need not denote a person at all.

Ten-line edition and reproducible evidence

The following records preserve manuscript lineation. “Source” is the selected normalized transcription, not a photograph or a claim of certain sign readings; “literal” is its unchanged mechanical decryption. The German and English rows are editorial layers. The underlying edition CSV also retains the inherited source and literal output. All source alternatives and reading grades are described below.

Apparatus: Source q at 12 and ÿ at 24 have independent visual support. Source t at 21 remains uncertain, hence final n in contentiren is marked. No payment, arrears or sum is stated.

The alphabetic evidence folder supplies the sign-by-sign selected alignment, edit ledger, defined alternatives and provenance. Reproduce the published arithmetic and branch checks with:

python targets/malsburg1637/alphabetic/alphabetic_verify.py

What the source choices change

Separate AI readings were made without supplying the proposed plaintext, followed by targeted rereading. Selection between ambiguous branches also used linguistic coherence; the selected 510-position stream is not therefore independent confirmation of every word. The difference ledger has 33 runs: 15 single-letter deletions, one insertion, one length-increasing u-to-ii replacement and 16 substitutions, for a net reduction of 13 positions. That accounting is not a unique reconstruction of scribal errors.

LocationSource choice or unresolved outputEffect and grade
Line 1, first code43 favored over 45 by comparison with the other 45 groups and the 3 in 1637M: better graphic support; no recovered referent
Line 3h/uM: nicht/nipht; selected h preserves phase
Line 4, sign 37Image reading w gives q in tqouppenI: Trouppen is conjectural; the source and literal layers retain w and q
Line 6g/qM: solche/solcho; selected g preserves phase
Line 7Overlap counts zero or one sign; a separate h/? remains provisionalM: zero-count branch selected; counting one shifts downstream phase
Line 8y/iy, n/r, and unknown blot extentM: selected one y and n give gefellig; r gives gefellng; the extra i changes downstream phase
Line 9m/n; image reading g at sign 42 gives itzigdnM: ihrem/khrem; I: itzigen is conjectural, with no corresponding source correction accepted
Line 10, sign 21t/?M: final n in contentiren remains provisional

The six binary choices (line 3 h/u, line 6 g/q, line 7 count, line 8 count, line 8 n/r, line 9 m/n) produce 64 defined branches: 16 with 510 positions, 32 with 511, and 16 with 512. schwedischengeneralen and descommendohalber survive all 64; the ending oglichkeitcontentirenlassen survives 16. These were already consulted anchors, not held-out evidence. The set does not enumerate every possible sign reading or the unknown blot extent, so it is not a probability model.

The residual tqouppen and itzigdn could each reflect a local encipherment shift one too small, but this remains conjecture. Changing the global key to 4,4,2,6,2 would also change 202 other letters. There is no accepted key alteration or silent repair of these words. In the repository’s conventions, disputed manuscript choices are M and conjectural restorations or grammatical supplies are I; H and C do not apply merely because arithmetic was verified.

Historical constraint, not recovered identity

Peter Melander is a documentary lead, not an identification of 43. Geyso, Beiträge, part III (1926), p. 99 n. 2, says that Melander sought “absolute Gewalt” through Malsburg, citing a letter from Malsburg at Wesel on 16 November 1636, old reference Kr. A. 1636, III. The phrase is in Geyso’s paraphrase; the original has not been inspected. It concerns authority to reward and punish officers and does not itself establish the disputed command relationship with Swedish generals.

Geyso pp. 115-117 and Hofmann pp. 68-69 also describe January retention offers to Melander. They weigh against an accomplished dismissal, although they can coexist with the conditional reading above. No inspected source connects a named man to both the distinctive command clause and code 43. The numerals 43 and 45 in other key alphabets are not name equations, and the later February and March letters have unresolved antecedents of their own.

The smallest useful next comparisons are the original letter of 16 November; the cipher sheets catalogued as LHA Koblenz 47/4400 (1635-1641, not inspected); and a possible Hoff report of 17/27 March 1637 in HStAM 4 f Staaten F, Frankreich 1297. The last could help trace the other LL-like occurrence, but neither the report’s presence nor common code values has been established. The January paragraph may copy another speaker’s intelligence; Malsburg’s authorship of the surrounding letter does not settle that question.

05 What remains uncertain

  • Codes. About forty three-digit groups and a few signs (193 tokens) have no value. Five are inferred from context and graded I: 128 = Cöln (the envoys “nach 128”), 130 = Hamburg (“apart zu 130 oder Lübeck”), 188 = die Staaten, 253 = Regiment, 273 = Compagnien. Others recur without a context that fixes them: 120 and 122 (persons at Kassel or in the field), 129, 140, 148, 176, 193, 212, and the name signs V, K, f, 4#, + and the paired capitals of ff. 32–33.
  • Single groups. About 200 groups read as written but give no word; most are the writer’s slips (a homophone of the wrong letter), which the scans confirm.
  • Pages in another hand. The French letter and extracts of ff. 32–33 use the same key with a few variant signs (a C-like sign for ai); 88% of their groups read.
  • The alphabetic block: its repeating key is recovered, but 43, 45 and LL remain without meanings or identified referents. The M-grade manuscript branches, I-grade editorial restorations, blot, singular er with plural wurden, and scope of the proposed letter remain explicit above. The annotation’s reading, expansion and attribution are separate unresolved questions.

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

ff. 3-4, 5/15 Jan 1637 read in part

Malsburg reports that he does not know where the garrisons' upkeep is to come from now that the contributions from the open country have ended, and urges that a safe-conduct for the envoys to the peace talks at <128 Cöln> be solicited from the King of Hungary. Block 'work/f003.txt' of reading_segmented.txt; f. 4 (address leaf) only in work/reading_auto.txt.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (89% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

HCP496_reading.md

# HCPortal 496 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, f. 3), Malsburg to the councillors at Kassel, 5/15 Jan 1637 — reading of the cipher

Rests on: `f003.txt` (Bourdeau's hand transcription of f. 3, one cipher line per scan line) decoded with `key.txt`
unchanged (`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`), the breaks checked on the HCPortal original scan
(hstam_4_h_1411_0003, 2946 x 2312, fetched 22 Oct 2026 into the session scratchpad; batch b08). No contemporary
decipherment on the leaf or the address side (f. 4), no clear copy, no edition (NOTES, Escalation). Code values:
`{Cöln}` = 128 (grade I, from context, `codes.txt`).

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b08): **read** (B); clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.667 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: open codes and signs. Log: `HCP496-509_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[201]` an unvalued code; `<+>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as the cipher gives it: the b08 normalisation (`research/reading_audit/b08_norm.py`) was undone on 23 Oct 2026 (batch r2b, `research/reading_audit/r2b_mals_denorm.py`: each word restored to the form its figures or the transcribed clear words give, e.g. alhir, Feint, schleinig, hette, dan, one); modern spelling only in the English file. Punctuation added. ⟨...⟩ = clear words written in the letter around the cipher.

## Reading

⟨… gemachet,⟩ das ich auch noch nit weis, wo hervor unsre Garnisonen hirnechst der Unterhalt her, so in Frucht ⟨und⟩ [201] bestehet, genommen werden soll; ⟨das⟩ die Contributiones vom platten Lande sowol alhir als in [239] ein Ende haben. ⟨Und⟩ wirt man nunmer alle Monat auf <+> [?…]tman [193] ⟨und⟩ <f> [273] an [210] zwölf <4#> Virtel casselische Virtel; weill die Unterofficirer duppelt Commis bekommen ⟨und⟩ [144] […] haben.

Es ist noch Zeit, das man sich beim König von Ungern angebe wegen der Fridenstractaten ⟨und⟩ salvum conductum vor <H#> Gesanten nach {Cöln} sollicitire. [176] wirt one [140] nit anfangen zu tractiren. Auf disem itzigem Zug [?steet] gar biel; derowegen Bettage hoch [?nötig] sein.

## Notes

- The first block follows the clear words "… gemachet," and ends before the clear sentence "Es sitzet auch itzo ein
  Capitain vom feind Doctor …"; the second block is the postscript written under "Der Herren Dienstwilliger".
- "unsre": the figures give u n P(ss) 13 r e (`unssre`); 13 is clear on the scan, so the writer doubled the s.
- "bestehet": `91 49 O 6? / 82 84 94` = be st ? / het; the group cut at the right edge of the line ("6?") must be an
  e-group (16 or 61), so the word is carried.
- "her, so in Frucht": the figures give `82 48 65` = her (82 is clear on the scan), not "der".
- `<+> 60 57 87 31` = <+> t m a n: the sign is a cross written like a t-stroke; a "Hauptman" or "Feldman" would need a
  value for it that no other occurrence confirms, so the word is left open.
- `[144] C 25 Y' X 9?`: after the code, a small c (not the capital mm sign), r, a dotted ei-sign, sch and a doubtful 9;
  no word comes out ("…reisch…"), so the clause is a hole.
- `<H#>` (crossed H) gives ll elsewhere; "vor ll Gesanten" is not a word, so the sign is open here.
- "steet": `O 48 7? 61 26` = st e 7 e t; the 7 sits against a blot; "stehet" would need a 72 (h) whose 2 is lost in the
  blot. "nötig": `83 7? 26 36 43` = n 7 t i g; the same 7 stands where ö is needed. One sign cannot be both h and ö, so
  both words are marked partly carried.
- "biel" = viel (b for v, as elsewhere in these letters).
Translation

HCP496_en.md

# HCPortal 496 (f. 3), Malsburg to the councillors at Kassel, 5/15 Jan 1637 — English

Translation of `HCP496_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b08), with the same holes: `[201]` an unvalued code, `<+>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; {Cologne} = code 128 valued from context. ⟨...⟩ = clear words.

## Translation

⟨… made,⟩ that I still do not know where our garrisons' upkeep is to be taken from hereafter, which consists in grain ⟨and⟩ [201]; ⟨since⟩ the contributions from the open country, both here and in [239], are at an end. ⟨And⟩ one will now every month [give?] to <+> […]tman [193] ⟨and⟩ <f> [273] in [210] twelve <4#> quarters, Kassel quarters; because the non-commissioned officers receive double rations ⟨and⟩ [144] […] have.

It is still time for one to present oneself to the King of Hungary about the peace negotiations ⟨and⟩ to solicit a safe-conduct for <H#> envoys to {Cologne}. [176] will not begin to negotiate without [140]. On this present campaign a great deal [?depends]; therefore days of prayer are [?most needful].

## Notes

- "Commis" = commissary bread, the soldier's ration. "Viertel" = the Kassel grain measure.
- "[give?]" is the translator's guess at the sense of the holed clause; the German has no verb carried.
- "King of Hungary": Ferdinand III, elected King of the Romans in December 1636.

ff. 12-13, Wesel, 7/17 Jan 1637 read in part

From Wesel: he does not know whether to go to <148> or to the army; the recruits and Dalwig's Verehrungsgelder, Eberstein's regiment, and the impossibility of travelling through Friesland with wagons and horses; word that it was decided at Regensburg to give the envoys to <128 Cöln> no safe-conduct, and of talks apart at <130 Hamburg> or Lübeck. Block 'work/f012.txt' of reading_segmented.txt.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (91% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

HCP497_reading.md

# HCPortal 497 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, ff. 12-13), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, Wesel, 7/17 Jan 1637 — reading of the numerical cipher

Rests on: `f012.txt` (Bourdeau's transcription of scan 0012, one cipher line per scan line; f. 13 is the address side,
no cipher) decoded with `key.txt` unchanged (`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`), the breaks checked on the HCPortal
original scan hstam_4_h_1411_0012 (2834 x 4284, fetched 23 Oct 2026 into the session scratchpad; batch r2b, log
`HCP497-506_reread_log.md`). No contemporary decipherment on the leaf or the address side, no clear copy, no edition
(NOTES, Escalation). The separate ten-line letter-cipher block at the foot of the page is another system and has its
own entry (`alphabetic/`). Code values: `{Cöln}` = 128, `{Hamburg}` = 130, `{Regiment}` = 253, `{die Staaten}` = 188
(grade I, from context, `codes.txt`).

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[148]` an unvalued code; `<XX>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[x]` a letter or word no figure carries; `[…]` unread. Spelling as the cipher gives it (u/v, i/j merged by the
key); nouns capitalised and punctuation added. ⟨...⟩ = clear words written in the letter around the cipher. The plain H
sign stands for ll (anstellen, alleine, soll er) and, as in the sibling letters, for tt (Mittel, mit-); it is read so
where only tt gives the word.

## Reading

⟨… so lang als möglich mainteniren werden,⟩ ich weis nun nicht, wie ich [?den] Weg anstellen [?soll], ⟨und⟩ ob <H#> noch in [148] oder bei der Armee sein [?…], eine Reisse noch vor sich gehen solte ⟨und⟩ <XX> meine Abfertigun[?g] [259] oder [258] schicken konten oder wolten; wurde ville Zeit [?…] ⟨das was ich …⟩

⟨… und H. Stadthalter annoch nicht wieder kommen,⟩ dass ich die Recrute ⟨und⟩ Vererungsgelder vor Dalwigs ⟨und⟩ Ebersteins {Regiment}, wie auch d[?ie] [?Leibguarde] mitnemen kan, ⟨als will ich bey des Herrn … abgeschickten botten⟩ und [?…] oder bei Zeigern {die Staaten} Ordre meines w[ei]tern Verhaltens, ob ich <6> alleine zu [129] zu kosten Mittel suchen oder <XX> alhir gelassen Train mitt bringen soll, erst erwarten; dan durch Friesslant mitt Wagen ⟨und⟩ Pferden zu gehen ist unmoglich. ⟨Wolten auch EfGl. bei …⟩

Es ist von guter Hant avisiret, dass man zu Regenspurg geschlossen, <H#> Gesanten nach {Cöln} keinen salvum conductum zu erteilen. [?Hiergegen] berichtet mich der Statthalter Nesselrot, dass der Bischoff von Wirzburg an ihn im Augustto geschriben, wen [129] beim Konig von Bomen, nunmer aber dem Romischen Konige, einkemen, diselben noch woll einen guten Friden erhalten solten. Mitt der [140] wurde man durch [137] apart zu {Hamburg} oder Lubeck tracti[r]en lassen. ⟨Es ist in den Spanischen guarnisonen …⟩

## Notes

- Block 1 follows the clear words "… so lang als möglich mainteniren werden,"; block 2 follows "… und H. Stadthalter
  annoch nicht wieder kommen,"; block 3 stands under the date "Datum Wesel den 7/17 Januarij Ao 1637" and before the
  signature flourish.
- "den Weg anstellen soll": the figures give `16 63 98 84 54 …` = en weg (no d-group at the head of the line) and
  `35 N` = so-ch at the end; read on the scan as written, so both words are partly carried.
- "sein [?…], eine Reisse": `… 68 G 83 37 48 | G 83 49 30 Y P 61` = s ei n w e | ei n e r ei ss e. "w e" gives no
  word before "eine Reisse"; left open.
- "Abfertigun[?g]": the last group of the line is a doubtful "5?" where a g-group is needed.
- "wurde ville Zeit [?…]": `29 49 18 | 84 83` = g e w | e n, then the clear text; no word ("gewinnen" would need
  more groups).
- "d[?ie] [?Leibguarde]": `27 9? | Y 10 34 71 39 97 27 84` = d 9 | ei b g u a r d e; the 9 is doubtful and no l-group
  stands before "eib".
- "und [?…] oder bei Zeigern": `71 83 27 94 70 42 7?` = u n d t a m 7; after "und" no word.
- "w[ei]tern Verhaltens, ob ich": `37 L 94 16 97 83` = w tt t e r n; `… 74 35 76 66 D` = s o b i ch (the s closes
  "Verhaltens"). `6?` before "alleine" is an unexplained figure.
- "Hiergegen": `82 16 25 66 | 49 43 48 63` = h e r i | e g e n.
- "diselben": the e is the figure 16 written above the line ("dis{16}lben").
- "tracti[r]en": `… 80 60 66 63 49 83` = c t i n e n; the scan (block 3, last line) shows 63 (n) where an r-group is
  needed, so the r is bracketed.
- "Mitt der [140]": `42 66 H 46 49 97` = m i <H> d e r; the scan shows the crossed H, read tt here.
Translation

HCP497_en.md

# HCPortal 497 (f. 12), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, Wesel, 7/17 Jan 1637 — English

Translation of `HCP497_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, batch r2b), with the same holes: `[148]` an unvalued code, `<XX>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; {Cologne} = 128, {Hamburg} = 130, {regiment} = 253, {the
States} = 188 valued from context. ⟨...⟩ = clear words. The letter-cipher block at the foot of the page is translated
in `alphabetic/`.

## Translation

⟨… will maintain as long as possible,⟩ I do not now know how I [?should] set about the journey, ⟨and⟩ whether <H#>
[should] still be at [148] or with the army [?…], [whether] a journey should still go ahead, ⟨and⟩ <XX> [could or
would] send my [?dispatch] [259] or [258]; much time would be [?…] ⟨that what I …⟩

⟨… and the Lord Statthalter has not yet come back,⟩ that I can take with me the recruits ⟨and⟩ the gratuities for
Dalwig's ⟨and⟩ Eberstein's {regiment}, as well as [?the] [?life guard], ⟨I will, by the messengers sent by the Lord
…,⟩ and [?…] or by the bearer [wait for] {the States'} order for my further conduct, whether I <6> should seek means
alone at [129] for the costs, or bring the train left here with me; for to go through Friesland with wagons ⟨and⟩
horses is impossible. ⟨Would Your Princely Grace also …⟩

It is reported from a good hand that it was decided at Regensburg to give <H#> envoys to {Cologne} no safe-conduct.
[?On the other hand] the Statthalter Nesselrode tells me that the Bishop of Würzburg wrote to him in August that, if
[129] came to terms with the King of Bohemia, but now with the King of the Romans, they should still obtain a good
peace. With the [140] one would have [137] negotiate separately at {Hamburg} or Lübeck. ⟨In the Spanish garrisons it
is …⟩

f. 12 foot, the alphabetic block of 7/17 Jan 1637 (Larry Beck with ChatGPT) read in part

A separate ten-line block in a letter cipher with repeating shifts 4, 5, 3, 6, 2, deciphered by Larry Beck with ChatGPT; the selected reading discusses command, the Swedish generals, the reciprocal joining of forces and the authority requested. A working edition: the code labels 43, 45 and LL are unidentified.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (91% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

# Malsburg, Wesel, 7/17 January 1637 (HCPortal 497, image 0012): the alphabetic block, reading

The "Editorial German" of `alphabetic/alphabetic_reading.md` (Larry Beck with ChatGPT, 2 Oct 2026; repeating
additive key 4 5 3 6 2, selected 510-position branch), copied here alone so that it is scored and translated without
the method, tables and apparatus, 23 Oct 2026 (translation wave v7). Their marks: `[43]`, `[45]`, `[LL]` code labels
not identified; `[x]`, `[word?]`, `[a/b]` uncertain signs or an interpretation; `†word†` an unresolved literal form;
`[BLOT]` a blot of unknown extent. Spacing, capitals and punctuation are editorial; the letters are the selected
literal output. German.

## Reading

Der [43] stehet itzo aufm Sprunge. Wollen [45] seiner gern los sein und ihn entbehren konnen, so ist es nun Zeit, das [LL] schreiben: weil sie sehen, das er mit den Schwedischen Generalen des Commendo halber sich nicht uergleichen †wurden†, [45] Estat aber erforderte, dat sie ihre †tqouppen† [Trouppen?] bey die Schweden und die Schweden wider bey sie stossen musten, auch [LL] actiones ihme nit mer gefielen, und uber das ihme solche absolute Gewalt, wie er begerte, nit geben konten, so stelleten sie es dahin, wa[BLOT]s ihme zu thun [gefellig/gefellng]; wolten ihn auch wider seine Gelegenheit nit aufhalten, sondern ihn nach [ihrem/khrem] †itzigdn† [itzigen?] Estat und Moglichkeit contentire[n?] lassen.
Translation

alphabetic_block_en.md

# Malsburg, Wesel, 7/17 January 1637 (HCPortal 497, image 0012): the alphabetic block, English translation

Translation (Claude Opus 5.5, 23 Oct 2026) of `alphabetic/alphabetic_block_reading.md`, the contributors' editorial
German of the ten-line block. Same marks: `[43]`, `[45]`, `[LL]` unidentified code labels; `[?word]` an uncertain
sign or interpretation; `†word†` an unresolved literal form (translated as far as it carries, kept between daggers);
`[BLOT]` a blot. The contributors' own cautious English, with their alternatives, is in `alphabetic_reading.md`.

## Translation

The [43] is now on the point of leaving. If [45] wish to be rid of him and can do without him, it is now time for
[LL] to write: since they see that he †would† not come to agreement with the Swedish generals over the command, but
the state of [45] required that they join their †troops† [?troops] to the Swedes and the Swedes in turn to them, and
also [LL]'s actions no longer pleased him, and moreover they could not give him such absolute authority as he
demanded, they left it to what [?pleased] him to do; nor would they keep him back against his convenience, but would
have him satisfied according to [?their] †present† [?present] state and ability.

f. 14, Jan 1637 decoded, not read

The provisioning of Hermannstein (Ehrenbreitstein); Obrist Hoffman's troops; marches by Marburg, Corbach and Brilon on the road from <128 Cöln>; Jan de Werth at Cologne. Block 'work/f014.txt' of reading_segmented.txt.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

HCP502_reading.md

# HCPortal 502 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, f. 14), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, Wesel, 16/26 Jan 1637 — reading of the cipher

Rests on: `f014.txt` (transcription agent, checked by Bourdeau) decoded with `key.txt` unchanged
(`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`), the breaks checked on the HCPortal original scan (hstam_4_h_1411_14, 2854 x
4200, fetched 22 Oct 2026 into the session scratchpad; batch b08). The right edge of the sheet is torn and folded: the
last group of many lines is partly or wholly lost (marked `?` in the transcription). No contemporary decipherment, no
clear copy, no edition. Code values: `{Cöln}` = 128, `{Regiment}` = 253 (grade I, `codes.txt`).

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b08): decoded, not read; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.167 (before 0.143); holes: open codes and signs in nearly every sentence, torn edge. Log: `HCP496-509_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[145]` an unvalued code; `<+>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[word]` supplied (here only letters lost at the torn edge); `[…]` unread. Spelling as the cipher gives it: the b08 normalisation (`research/reading_audit/b08_norm.py`) was undone on 23 Oct 2026 (batch r2b, `research/reading_audit/r2b_mals_denorm.py`: each word restored to the form its figures or the transcribed clear words give, e.g. alhir, Feint, schleinig, hette, dan, one); modern spelling only in the English file.
Punctuation added. ⟨...⟩ = clear words written in the letter around the cipher.

## Reading

⟨… erwarten,⟩ [?hoffe] aber doch noch am Ende dises Monats, wofern mir das Wette[r], der Weg ⟨und⟩ [145] newe Werbungen in [235] daran nit verhind[e]rlich sein, mich an bestimte Ort neben [XX] Hoffstat [?einzustellen].

⟨Den 15/25 huius⟩ ist die Proviandirung Hermansteins [?vorgenommen], ⟨und⟩ füret Obristl Hoffman das Volck, deme […]molbram, Captain Helwigsch mit vom Roten ⟨und⟩ Adria von Carpens [191] mitt <f> au man [193] neben <o> {Regiment}stucken mi[t]gegeben. Der [200] seint etwa <K> <4#> ⟨und⟩ <m> [?gewesen]; kommen i[n] [262] Tagen schwerlich dahin. Haben iren Weg […] Molheim über die [266] auf Siburg ⟨und⟩ <4#> die rechte Stra[ss]e von {Cöln} auf [189] genommen, ⟨und⟩ nit mer als <+> au casselisch[e] Virtel [210] bei sich.

⟨Jan de Wert ist in persona zu Cölln und hat sein Volck in den Stifft Münster gelegt;⟩ wirt ihnen one Zweiffel nachgehen. Wen das [?geschicht], so wirt Obristl Hoffman [?die Lohngerat] hin auf Marpurg ⟨und⟩ [215] oder Corbach, Brilen [138] ⟨und⟩ so wide[r] seine Garnison gehen. ⟨Gott gebe ihnen Glück,⟩ das es sowol als […] Hanau [?abgehe].

[SS] hat mit eigen Handen an seiner geheimbten Rahte [?geschrieben], ⟨dass sich nun recht alle Gemüter … zum Fürsten …⟩.

⟨… dazu dan⟩ er und sei[n] Her[r] [?Bruder] ⟨gute Beförderung thun wollen;⟩ ⟨welches alles⟩ auf die Unkosten nach [?Hermanstein] gehen. ⟨… sobald ich herfahre,⟩ wie es denen auf der […]e nach Hermanstein gehet, ⟨will ich … überschreiben.⟩

## Notes

- Letters in plain brackets are the values of groups lost or half lost at the torn right edge where one letter is
  certain from the word (Wette[r], verhind[e]rlich, mi[t]gegeben, i[n], Stra[ss]e, wide[r], sei[n]); a lost group that
  the word does not fix is `[…]`.
- "deme […]molbram": `27 61 56 48 3? / 57 93 15 76 97 55 42`; the last group of l. 12 is cut (a 30-group); no name
  "…molbram" is known to us, so the hole stays.
- `neben o 253 ST 47 80 92 61 83` = "neben o Regimentstucken": a small o with a point before the code, not a figure
  group; open.
- "gewesen": the figures give `ge a e sen` (28 = a, where a w-group is needed); partly carried.
- "die Lohngerat hin": `27 66 84 99 73 21 22 29 49 97 87 96 82 66 63` = dielohngerathin; the sense of "Lohngerat" is not
  clear to us.
- "abgehe": `55 7? 29 16 21 79` = a 7 gehe; the lone 7 (also in HCPortal 496) has no value in the key.
- "geschrieben": `… 18 / 49 63 54 16 X 66 61 …` = "w / enge schrieben"; the writer seems to have begun a word at the line
  end; the participle is carried, the w-group is not.
- "Bruder": `21 48 65 10 46 24 46 61 25` = her b d u d e r; "Herr Bruder" with d for r once; partly carried.
- "Hermanstein" in block 4: `82 84 25 42 39 63 M Y ?` = herman + mm-sign + ei + cut group; the mm-sign stands where st
  is needed.
Translation

HCP502_en.md

# HCPortal 502 (f. 14), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, Wesel, 16/26 Jan 1637 — English

Translation of `HCP502_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b08), with the same holes: `[145]` an unvalued code, `<+>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; {Cologne} = 128, {regiment} = 253 valued from context.
⟨...⟩ = clear words. Partial reading: the document stays "decoded, not read".

## Translation

⟨… await,⟩ but I [?hope] still at the end of this month, provided the weather, the road ⟨and⟩ [145] new recruitments in [235] do not hinder it, to [?present myself] at the appointed place with [XX] household.

⟨On the 15/25th of this month⟩ the provisioning of Hermanstein was [?undertaken], ⟨and⟩ Lieutenant-Colonel Hoffman leads the troops, to whom […]molbram, Captain Helwigsch with vom Roten ⟨and⟩ Adria von Carpens [191] with <f> au man [193] besides <o> {regimental} guns were given. The [200] were about <K> <4#> ⟨and⟩ <m>; they will hardly get there in [262] days. They took their way […] Mülheim over the [266] to Siegburg ⟨and⟩ <4#> the right road from {Cologne} to [189], ⟨and⟩ have no more than <+> au Kassel quarters [210] with them.

⟨Jan de Werth is at Cologne in person and has put his troops into the Bishopric of Münster;⟩ he will no doubt follow them. If that [?happens], Lieutenant-Colonel Hoffman will go [?…] to Marburg ⟨and⟩ [215] or Korbach, Brilon [138], ⟨and⟩ so back to his garrison. ⟨God give them luck,⟩ that it [?turns out] as well as […] Hanau.

[SS] has [?written] with his own hand to his privy councillors ⟨that all minds now … to the prince …⟩.

⟨… for which⟩ he and his [?brother] ⟨will give good help;⟩ ⟨all of which⟩ goes on the costs for [?Hermanstein]. ⟨… as soon as I go there,⟩ how it goes with those on the […] to Hermanstein, ⟨I shall … write.⟩

## Notes

- Hermanstein = Ehrenbreitstein, the Hessian-held fortress on the Rhine, then blockaded.

ff. 15-17, Jan 1637 decoded, not read

No audience yet; notice of the end of the Stillstand; companies and dragoons, with recruiting patents already issued; the ambassador of <188 die Staaten>; money and horses. Blocks 'work/f015.txt' and 'work/f016.txt' of reading_segmented.txt; f. 17 only in work/reading_auto.txt.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# HCPortal 503 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, ff. 15-16), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, Wesel, Jan 1637 — reading of the cipher

Rests on: `f015.txt` (the "Triplicat", one faint block) and `f016.txt` (the letter of 30 Jan [/9 Feb] 1637, two blocks),
transcription agents checked by Bourdeau, decoded with `key.txt` unchanged (`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`), the
breaks checked on the HCPortal original scans (hstam_4_h_1411_0015, 0016; fetched 22 Oct 2026 into the session
scratchpad; batch b08). f. 16 has heavy show-through from the enclosure list on the other side. f. 17 is the address
side (no cipher). No contemporary decipherment, no clear copy, no edition. Code values: `{Compagnien}` = 273,
`{die Staaten}` = 188 (grade I, `codes.txt`).

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b08): decoded, not read; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.375 (raw decode 0.389); holes: open codes and signs in most sentences. Log: `HCP496-509_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[120]` an unvalued code; `<H#>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as the cipher gives it: the b08 normalisation (`research/reading_audit/b08_norm.py`) was undone on 23 Oct 2026 (batch r2b, `research/reading_audit/r2b_mals_denorm.py`: each word restored to the form its figures or the transcribed clear words give, e.g. alhir, Feint, schleinig, hette, dan, one); modern spelling only in the English file. punctuation added. ⟨...⟩ = clear words
written in the letter around the cipher. Paragraphs: the f. 15 block (Triplicat); f. 16 block 1 (two paragraphs);
f. 16 block 2.

## Reading

⟨… ich habe ihm⟩ beim [122] u<4#> [?Resolution] auf [129] Schreiben angehalten. Es ist aber derselbe itz mit [120] wegen des Verlust vor Hermanstein in der […] ⟨und⟩ hatt die Gedancken ni<A#> alle bei einander.

⟨… weil er in Staadische protection genommen ist,⟩ [120] will noch von keinem [202] wissen; sagt, er erwarte Bauregart alle Tage wider. ⟨Und⟩ Hoff schreibet noch [?kürtzlich], das Borigart noch nie Audiens gehabt. Ich habe [120] andeiten [?lassen], das [129] nach Anfang des Traicte zu Minden ⟨und⟩ <+> Aufkündigung des Stilstants an die 9 {Compagnien} [193] ⟨und⟩ <f> ⟨und⟩ 9 {Compagnien} [212], wie auch sch[…] {Compagnien} Tragons [?schon] [?…]teil [?geworben], ⟨und⟩ 2 Teils zu werben Patente albereits ausgeteilt [?hetten]. Solten sie nun in irer voller Werbung [?gehindert] werden, so würden sie in aller irer Intention ⟨und⟩ Actio[n] gehemmet, welches dan dem König ⟨und⟩ gemeinen Wesen mehr als {die Staaten} […] particulir Schaden bringen [würde].

Zu deme [?hette] Hoff [?berichtet], das ihm were [?angedeutet] worden, wie das der Ambassadeur schon Ordre [XX], die auf den leizten [?December] schiene <4H#> <o>ch Reichs[…]dalen solte, dan er noch [201] in Deitschlant ligen hätte. Er hat sich aber nirgend zu verstehen wollen ⟨und⟩ wirt bald von hir [?ziehen]. Sorge derwegen, man werde sich auf die Golder, vil weniger auf die [?abgeredete] Termine verlassen ⟨und⟩ […] machen kommen.

⟨Ich habe dieses … bis EfGl. ich einweinig⟩ vons <triangle> ⟨mein Meinung überschreiben können, und dünket mich, es werde EfGl. nicht abhanden gehen.⟩ Er redet aber nirgend von Friedmachen. Sagt, er wolte einen Finger [?auf] der F[au]st drumb geben, dass <H#> den [?bewussten] Brieff von hir vor [120] Ankunft weggeschicket hätte; dann ihn deuchte, {die Staaten} er alsdan mit viel ruhigerem Gemüth dienen könnte. Zu dem wirt er über <e> Pferde notwendig mitbringen müssen. Er sihet aber itzo keine Gelegenheit, zu [129] zu kommen.

## Notes

- "Bauregart", "Borigart": `76 70 S 25 61 43 39 30 26` and `76 93 25 69 43 39 30 26`, Beauregard twice, in two spellings.
- "Audiens": `nie F# 27 69 84 83 77` = nie + crossed-F sign + diens; F# is valued au in the key.
- "lassen": `15 39 T 61 22` = la + ff-sign + en; partly carried.
- "schon … teil geworben": `X 12? 22? 94 G 78 / 43 79 98 73 25 9 61 22` = sch p n t ei l / gewor 9 en; the 9 must be b; the
  word before "teil" is not carried.
- "hetten": `82 61 L? …` the l. 16-17 run is "he <H#> / en"; the crossed H here stands where tt is needed, as again in
  "he <H#> e" (= hette) in l. 27; elsewhere on the page it gives ll (wi<H#> = will, a<H#>e = alle, fo<H#>er = voller,
  wo<H#>en = wollen). Its value is not one letter pair, so the words that need tt are marked partly carried.
- "hette Hoff berichtet": `21 16 L 89 82 35 B 76 61 25 66 N 26 C 61 94` = he tt o hoff bericht mm et; o for e and a stray
  mm-sign.
- "Ambassadeur": `28 56 [blotted group] 10 39 W 55 32 79 58 65` = am [blot] bassadeur.
- "ziehen": `98 36 21 48 63` = wihen; w for z.
- "Golder": a payment term ("auf die Golder, vil weniger auf die abgeredete Termine"); partly understood.
- "würde" in "Schaden bringen [würde]" is supplied: the run ends at "bringen".
- f. 15 "in der […]": `27 16 30 37 70 10 61 97 71 44 48 72 55 / L` = der waberuneha tt; no word comes out.
Translation

# HCPortal 503 (ff. 15-16), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, Wesel, Jan 1637 — English

Translation of `HCP503_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b08), with the same holes: `[120]` an unvalued code, `<H#>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread; {companies} = 273, {the States} = 188
valued from context. ⟨...⟩ = clear words. Partial reading: the document stays "decoded, not read".

## Translation

⟨… I have⟩ pressed [122] for a [?resolution] on [129]'s letter. But he is now, with [120], because of the loss before Hermanstein, in the […] ⟨and⟩ does not have his wits together.

⟨… because he has been taken into the States' protection,⟩ [120] will not yet hear of any [202]; he says he expects Beauregard back any day. ⟨And⟩ Hoff writes [?recently] that Beauregard has still had no audience. I have let [120] know that [129], after the opening of the treaty at Minden ⟨and⟩ <+> the notice ending the truce, to the 9 {companies} [193] ⟨and⟩ <f> ⟨and⟩ 9 {companies} [212], as also sch[…] {companies} of dragoons [?already] [?…] part [?recruited], ⟨and⟩ had already issued patents to recruit 2 parts. If they were now [?hindered] in their full recruiting, they would be checked in all their intention ⟨and⟩ action, which would bring more particular harm to the King ⟨and⟩ the common cause than to {the States} […].

Moreover Hoff [?reported] that he had been [?told] how the ambassador already had orders [XX], which on the last of [?December] appeared <4H#> <o>ch Reichs[…]taler should, since he still had [201] lying in Germany. But he would not commit himself anywhere ⟨and⟩ will soon [?leave] here. He fears, therefore, that one cannot rely on the payments, much less on the [?agreed] terms, ⟨and⟩ […] come to make.

⟨I wrote this … three days ago but did not want to close it until I could⟩ ⟨send Your Princely Grace my opinion a little; and it seems to me Your Princely Grace will not lose by it.⟩ But he speaks nowhere of making peace. He says he would give a finger [?of] his fist to have sent the [?said] letter away from here before [120]'s arrival; for it seemed to him that he could then serve {the States} with a much calmer mind. Moreover he will have to bring <e> horses with him of necessity. But he sees no opportunity at present to get to [129].

## Notes

- Beauregard: the French envoy expected at The Hague; Hoff: the Hessian agent whose reports are in HStAM 4 f Frankreich.
- "Golder": rendered "payments" (sums in gulden); the word is not certain.

f. 18, 14/24 Feb 1637 decoded, not read

Some of the <212> are so frozen that several have become cripples; the obligation over the loss before Hermannstein; the new recruiters. Block 'work/f018.txt' of reading_segmented.txt.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

HCP504_reading.md

# HCPortal 504 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, f. 18), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 14/24 Feb 1637 — reading of the cipher

Rests on: `f018.txt` (Bourdeau's transcription of scan 0018, one cipher line per scan line) decoded with `key.txt`
unchanged (`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`), the breaks checked on the HCPortal original scan hstam_4_h_1411_0018
(fetched 23 Oct 2026 into the session scratchpad; batch r2b, log `HCP497-506_reread_log.md`). No contemporary
decipherment, clear copy or edition (NOTES, Escalation). Code values: `{Compagnien}` = 273 (grade I, `codes.txt`).

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[212]` an unvalued code; `<V>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[x]` a letter or word no figure carries; `[…]` unread. Spelling as the cipher gives it (u/v, i/j merged by the
key); nouns capitalised and punctuation added. ⟨...⟩ = clear words. The plain H sign stands for ll (wil, wol er) and tt
(hatt), as in the sibling letters. The writer's mm-signs stand where "können" is needed ("thun kommen"); those words are
marked.

## Reading

⟨… nicht weit zu sehen /⟩ es seint aber die [212] ein Teils so erfroren, das auch etliche zu Kroppeln worden, ⟨und⟩ etliche Glidder haben mussen ablosen lassen; so seint auch viel ⟨ffreundt?⟩ hernach gestorben, ⟨und⟩ <L> zur <16#> he worden, das die Reiterei noch wol sobalt keinen Dinst wirt thun [?konnen]. So verstercken sich auch die {Compagnien} zu Fus noch schlecht, wie auch die newen Werbers, wie auch Beiligentz zu sehen. [?…] [122] hatt ⟨bis dato⟩ wegen dessen [?das] [120] die Obligation, so er wegen des Verlust, so sich etwa auf der Reise nach Hermanstein zutragen mochte, von sich gegeben, disputiret, nit nach dem [123] kommen [?konnen]. ⟨so hat er aber … nunmehr etliche tage abreisen und bald wieder kommen werden, gestalt ich ihr das fleissig⟩ nach <9#> [148] zu gehen anregen will, wiewol er gewaltige argumenta disuasoria in [260] zu verherren vorbringet ⟨und⟩ were ihme doch itzo durchzukommn unmoglich. ⟨weil der Gen: Zeugmeister …⟩

⟨… Und Oberst Steins Regiment … gegenwärtig geschickt /⟩ das [?übrige] [?wirklich] an Golt zu meiner ⟨gottlob⟩ glucklichen Uberkunft mitt bringen. Ich sorge, <H> haben sich des <V> an bewustem Ort nit zu versichern, dan er sich [?…] wirt einschliessen lassen. ⟨Thue E. F. G. hiermit in gottes gn. …⟩

## Notes

- "Kroppeln" (Krüppeln): `51 97 35 81? | 12 61 78 31` = k r o p | p e l n.
- "<L> zur <16#> he worden": after the clear "Und" a lone L-sign (tt) and a crossed 16 that give no word; the clause
  is open.
- "das die Reiterei noch wol": `27 11 79 97 Y 60 16 25 G 83 35 N` = d i e r ei t e r ei n o ch, checked on the scan:
  Reiterei, then noch (the September segmentation had split it "Reiter ein noch").
- "thun [?konnen]", "kommen [?konnen]": the figures give k o mm e n; the sense needs "können". Marked partly carried.
- "Beiligentz" = the enclosure (beiliegends): `76 Y | 78 36 54 79 31 26 52` = b ei | l i g e n t z.
- "[?…] [122] hatt": `77 84 30` = s e r before the code gives no word; `72 70 H` = h a <H>, read "hatt" (H = tt).
- "[?das]": `22 29? 28 77`: the scan has a g-shaped 29 where a d-group is needed.
- "wiewol er": `37 36 37 35 H 79 30` = w i w o <H> e r.
- "zu verherren" = zu verharren (the writer's e).
- "das [?übrige] [?wirklich] an Golt" (checked on the scan: 78, not 10): `27 70 77 71 78 79 97 69 43 61 37 30 15 11 N 39 44 29 73 | 15 41` = d a s u l e r
  i g e w r l i ch a n g o | l t: "ulerige" for "übrige" (78 l where a b-group is needed) and "wrlich" have no
  certain reading; "an Golt" is carried.
- "Ich sorge, <H> haben": the H stands alone, no word; "an bewustem Ort" = `70 22 10 | 16 37 88 Z 16 56 73 25 26` = a n b | e w u st e m o r t.
- "dan er sich [?…]": `1? 18 15 | 30 99 66 Z?` = a lone stroke, w l r l i st: no word.
Translation

# HCPortal 504 (f. 18), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 14/24 Feb 1637 — English

Translation of `HCP504_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, batch r2b), with the same holes: `[212]` an unvalued code, `<V>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; {companies} = 273 valued from context. ⟨...⟩ = clear words.
Partial reading: the document stays "decoded, not read".

## Translation

⟨… not far to see /⟩ but some of the [212] are so frozen that several have become cripples, ⟨and⟩ several have had to
have limbs taken off; and many ⟨friends?⟩ have died afterwards, ⟨and⟩ <L> to <16#> [...] become, so that the cavalry
will hardly be able to do any service soon. The {companies} on foot are also filling up badly, as are the new
recruiters, as the enclosure shows. [?…] [122] has ⟨until now⟩, because [?of the fact that] [120] disputes the
obligation he gave concerning the loss that might occur on the journey to Hermanstein, not [?been able] to come to the
[123]. ⟨but he has … now leave for some days and come back soon, so that I urge her diligently⟩ to go to <9#> [148],
although he puts forward powerful dissuasive arguments for staying in [260] ⟨and⟩ it would in any case be impossible
for him to get through now. ⟨because the General Master of the Ordnance …⟩

⟨… And Colonel Stein's regiment … sent at present /⟩ [to] bring with me [?the rest] [?really] in gold at my ⟨thank
God⟩ happy arrival. I fear <H> [they] cannot make sure of the <V> at the known place, for he will let himself be shut
in [?…]. ⟨Commend Your Princely Grace herewith to God's grace …⟩

ff. 23-24, 7/17 Feb 1637 decoded, not read

The dispute over whether Hermannstein was effectively provisioned; ransom and gifts for the commanders; the finding that 'we have dealt with a Frenchman'; peace talks in Picardy with nobody sent to <128>; a separate peace with the imperial commissioners; the garrisons ruined. Blocks 'work/f023.txt' and 'work/f024.txt' of reading_segmented.txt.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Reading

HCP505_reading.md

# HCPortal 505 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, ff. 23-24), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 7/17 Feb 1637 — reading of the cipher

Rests on: `f023.txt` and `f024.txt` (Bourdeau's transcription of scans 0023 and 0024, one cipher line per scan line)
decoded with `key.txt` unchanged (`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`), the breaks checked on the HCPortal original
scans hstam_4_h_1411_0023 and 0024 (fetched 23 Oct 2026 into the session scratchpad; batch r2b, log
`HCP497-506_reread_log.md`). No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition (NOTES, Escalation). Code values:
`{Cöln}` = 128, `{die Staaten}` = 188 (grade I, `codes.txt`).

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[120]` an unvalued code; `<f>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[x]` a letter or word no figure carries; `[…]` unread. Spelling as the cipher gives it (u/v, i/j merged by the
key); nouns capitalised and punctuation added. ⟨...⟩ = clear words. The crossed signs: Z# = st (Estat, Stillstant), as
the plain Z; 7 = st (gestalt), as in f. 28. "1 t. Reichst." = 1 thousand Reichsthaler (t = tausend, as written).

## Reading

⟨… durch beygelegte verpflichtung heraus gegeben,⟩ welche er aber nunmer aus dem Fundament, das Hermanstein nit effectivement proviandiret sei, disputiret ⟨und⟩ nichts [?gestehen] wil. Wir aber vermeinen, dass das Wort effectivement nur die Vergeltunge vor die Befelshaber angehe, weil seine Hantschrift <f> Gelidder hette; dan[?n] verpflichtet er sich, alles, sowol Reit- als wegen Pferte, so im hin ⟨und⟩ herzihen drauf gehen, zu bezalen, [?alle] Gefangene zu losen, <f> Verehrungen zu geben an die Befelshaber, so das irige auf der Reise thun wur[d]en. Er ist aber ⟨bis dato⟩ nirgents zuzubringen, sondern weiset die ungestime Furleite zu bezalen uns an. [?…] beit sich zwar 1 t. Reichst. auf Rechnung herzugeben, [?doch] der Gestalt, das, wen der Konig die Kosten nit bezalen wolte, [?selbige] alsdan an den erschienenten 5 t. Reichst. abgekurtzet werden solten. Wir [?…] weise solsche nit annemen do[r]ffen, sondern halten davor, das er [?vermoge] Verschreibung allen Schaden zu zalen schuldig sei. Stecken also [?in der] Bruhe, wissen nit, womit wir ihn zwingen wollen, ⟨und⟩ befinden nun in [d]er That, das wir mit einem Frantzosen gehan[d]elt haben.

⟨Befehl habe ich auch⟩ bei [120] durch den [122] ⟨und⟩ Scheffern umb Erlegung der im verflossenen Decemb. fellig gewesene <H#> t. Reichst. ⟨:⟩ damit man die [?angenomene] [?newe] Werbungen mochten aussfuren, aller Hant Krieksnotturft in Zeiten herbeischa[ffen] ⟨und⟩ den O[b]risten Wardenberg mit seinem [191] auch nemen konte; dan {die Staaten} Estat auf die Gelder gemacht ⟨und⟩ schon Ordre, wozu sie angewend[e]t [w]erden solten, gegeben hetten. ⟨gefangen gehalten, darauf gibt er gar antwort, dass er … tage⟩ der Assignation von [?…] ⟨gegenwärtig werden?, Und wenn man⟩ ihn fragt, ob man dan auch auf den kunftigen ersten Marte der <S> t. Reichst. gewis mechtig sein konte, so zihet man die Schuldern; sorge al[?s]o, man auf die Gelder kein gewissen Estat zu machen habe ⟨Und solches Und so viel destomehr weil ein starck geraidt gehet⟩, als [?…] einen Stillstant auf <n> Monat gemacht hette ⟨und⟩ das <F> <F> ⟨und⟩ [189] zu [?Artret] in Piccardi zusammen kommen ⟨und⟩ daselbst Frieden tr[a]ctiren solten; dan zu {Cöln} horet man von keinem Gesanten, der deswegen dahin kommen solte.

⟨Will man auch sagen⟩ Baspons de la Guarne zu Stettin ankommen sein ⟨und⟩ Commission auf Marggraff Sigmunten von Brandenburg wegen der [140] mit den Kaisserlichen Commissarien, als dem [137] [163] ⟨und⟩ [177], einen Frieden apart zu tractiren, mitt bracht haben solte. ⟨Da das aber … E. F. G. die beste nachricht haben werden,⟩ unssere Garnisonen seint also verderbet, das die [200] notwentig weggehen ⟨und⟩ man den Soldaten Commis ⟨und⟩ halb [202] hinfro geben mus; darzu aber ein grosser Verlag ge[?h]oret. ⟨Dessen wegen wir auch des Obristen Wartenberg … auf E. F. G. discretion mitbringen wolle,⟩ weil es mit de[m] Reiderlant nit angehen wirt ⟨/⟩ nit accommodiren [?konnen]. ⟨das unsere guarnisonen gar überlegt, und alle newe werber ausserhalb …⟩

⟨… ist er zwar nicht abgeneigt, allein⟩ er weis nit, wie er one Schaden ⟨und⟩ mitt guten Reden aus frantzosischen Diensten kommen wil. ⟨… weiter quartier haben könte,⟩ die Schweden ⟨, oder⟩ <0> [191] ander [269] ⟨oder⟩ in [M]eckelburg unterhalten wollten. ⟨So könte man … Regiment⟩ alhie einzuschi<8>en, zu Winsen wider an das Lant zu setzen ⟨und⟩ nach seinen Cuartiren oder der Schwedischen Armee gehen zu la<V>en. Solcher Ge7alt konte man auch die e[217] nach der [269], wen sie auf Newenburg ⟨und⟩ durch das [231] wegen der Leine ⟨und⟩ aller nit kommen konten, bringen [?…] ⟨Gleich im schreiben werde ich berichtet …⟩

## Notes

- f. 23 block 1 follows "… durch beygelegte verpflichtung heraus gegeben,"; the cipher runs on to the top of f. 24
  ("…burg wegen der [140] …"); f. 24 block 2 follows "… ist er zwar nicht abgeneigt, allein".
- "[?gestehen]": `13 79 48 O 61 21 49 63` = (nicht)s e e st e h e n: an e-group where g is needed.
- "<f> Gelidder": the underlined f-sign, no value (also before "Verehrungen").
- "dan[?n] verpflichtet": `55 31 P 71 48 30 12 23` = a n ss u e r p f: the ss-sign after "dan" gives no word.
- "[?alle] Gefangene": `O? 62 H 79` = st a ll e (the st-sign doubtful in the transcription): "stalle" gives no word.
- "uns an. [?…] beit sich": `88 44 74 39 83 77 Y 42 49 30 | 76 Y 60 74 66 N` = u n s a n s ei m e r | b ei t s i ch,
  checked on the scan: "seimer" gives no word ("er beut sich" is the sense; the letters do not give it). "1 t. Reichst."
  = `1 60 25 Y D 74 26` on the scan.
- "[?doch]": the line-final `3` (on the scan a 3 with a blot) then `35 N` = o ch: a 3 where a d-group is needed.
- "[?selbige]": `37 35 99 26 49 79 84 15 10 11 54 16` = w o l t e e e l b i g e, checked on the scan: an e-group
  where s is needed.
- "Wir [?…] weise": `18 66 97 10 10 16 25 24 23 40 11 16 | 37 Y 13 84` = w i r b b e r u f z i e | w ei s e,
  checked on the scan; "aber auf die Weise" would need a, a and d where the figures give b, (none) and z (the writer
  puts a z-group for d also in "zer" and "gehanzelt" in this letter). Left open.
- "[?vermoge] Verschreibung": `58 61 75 {struck 42? 73} 29 48` = u e o (struck m o) g e: the writer struck two groups;
  "vermoge" needs an r.
- "[?in der] Bruhe": `11 49 32 61 25` = i e d e r: no n-group.
- "in [d]er That", "gehan[d]elt": `11 31 52 16 25`, `83 40 61 15 94`: a z-group (52, 40) stands where d is needed.
- "fellig gewesene <H#> t. Reichst.": `… 61 H#? 60 25 Y D 13 60`: the crossed H before "t Reichst" is a figure sign, no value read.
- "[?angenomene] [?newe] Werbungen": `70 63 43 16 22 84 31 16 18 49 98 48 30 76 …` = a n g e n e n e w e w e r b …:
  the letters give "angenene wewerbungen".
- "angewend[e]t [w]erden": `61 22 32 27 26 19 61 25 27 79 83` = e n d d t c e r d e n.
- "Estat" = `79 Z# 70 60`; "Stillstant" = `Z# 66 H Z 70 63 26` (crossed Z = st as the plain Z).
- "als [?…] einen": `39 15 13 37 70 84 G G Y 22 48 83` = a l s w a e ei ei ei n e n: no word before "einen".
- "[?Artret]": `39 30 26 25 79 94` = a r t r e t, perhaps a place in Picardy; not identified.
- "tr[a]ctiren": `94 97 89 80 96?` = t r o c t: an o-group where a is needed.
- "Schuldern, sorge al[?s]o": `X 71 15 27 48 30 44 77 35 97 29 49 39 15 19? 73`; the c-group 19 is doubtful.
- "<S> t. Reichst.", "das <F> <F>": the au-signs S and F stand alone where no "au" fits.
- "Soldaten": the crossed 39 (a) between d and t.
- "weil es mit de[m] Reiderlant": `98 Y [blot] 15 16 13 56 11 60 32 61 {struck 57} 25 G 46 84 97 99 70 22 41` = w ei
  [blot] l e s m i t d e (struck m) r ei d e r l a n t: the writer struck the m of "dem"; the Reiderland (East
  Frisia) is named again on f. 28.
- "[M]eckelburg": the writer struck a 57 (m) and wrote "eckelburg"; the m is supplied.
- "einzuschi<8>en": a large 8 where ff is needed (sense "einzuschiffen"), left as the sign.
- "la<V>en": the triangle sign stands where ss is needed.
- "bringen [?…]": the cipher ends with an sch-sign before the clear text.
Translation

HCP505_en.md

# HCPortal 505 (ff. 23-24), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 7/17 Feb 1637 — English

Translation of `HCP505_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, batch r2b), with the same holes: `[120]` an unvalued code, `<f>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; {Cologne} = 128, {the States} = 188 valued from context.
⟨...⟩ = clear words. Partial reading: the document stays "decoded, not read".

## Translation

⟨… given out by the enclosed undertaking,⟩ which he now disputes on the ground that Hermanstein was not effectively
provisioned, ⟨and⟩ he will [?admit] nothing. But we hold that the word "effectivement" concerns only the reward for
the commanders, because his written undertaking had <f> articles; for he binds himself to pay for everything spent on
riding and on horses in going to and fro, to ransom [?all] prisoners, to give <f> gratuities to the commanders who do
their part on the journey. But ⟨until now⟩ he cannot be brought to anything, but refers us to paying the importunate
carters. [?…] he offers indeed to advance 1 thousand Reichsthaler on account, [?but] on condition that, if the King
will not pay the costs, [?the same] shall then be deducted from the 5 thousand Reichsthaler that have fallen due. We
[?…] may not accept that in this way, but hold that he is bound [?by] his undertaking to pay all the damage. So we are
[?in the] soup, and do not know how we are to compel him, ⟨and⟩ find now in fact that we have dealt with a Frenchman.

⟨I have also an order⟩ by [120] through the [122] ⟨and⟩ Scheffer, for payment of the <H#> thousand Reichsthaler that
fell due last December, ⟨:⟩ so that the [?recruitments] [?undertaken] might be carried out, all kinds of war supplies
brought in in good time ⟨and⟩ Colonel Wardenberg with his [191] also taken on; for {the States} had made an estimate
on the money ⟨and⟩ had already given orders for what it should be used for. ⟨held prisoner; to which he gives the
answer that he … days⟩ of the assignment of [?…] ⟨present?, and when one⟩ asks him whether one could be sure of the
<S> thousand Reichsthaler on the first of March next, they put off the debts; [I] fear therefore that one can make no
sure estimate on the money ⟨and this all the more because a strong rumour goes round⟩, as [?…] had made a truce for
<n> months ⟨and⟩ that the <F> <F> ⟨and⟩ [189] were to meet at [?Artret] in Picardy ⟨and⟩ negotiate peace there; for
at {Cologne} one hears of no envoy who was to come there for that purpose.

⟨It is also said⟩ that Baspons de la Guarne has arrived at Stettin ⟨and⟩ is said to have brought a commission for
Margrave Sigmund of Brandenburg on behalf of the [140], to negotiate a separate peace with the imperial commissaries,
namely the [137] [163] ⟨and⟩ [177]. ⟨Since Your Princely Grace will have the best information of it,⟩ our garrisons
are so ruined that the [200] must necessarily leave ⟨and⟩ one must from now on give the soldiers bread ⟨and⟩ half
[202]; but that requires a large outlay. ⟨For that reason we also … Colonel Wartenberg … would bring at Your Princely
Grace's discretion,⟩ because with the Reiderland it will not work ⟨/⟩ [they] [?cannot] be accommodated. ⟨that our
garrisons are greatly overburdened, and all new recruiters outside …⟩

⟨… he is indeed not unwilling, only⟩ he does not know how he can come out of French service without loss ⟨and⟩ with
good words. ⟨… could have further quarters,⟩ the Swedes ⟨, or⟩ <0> [191] other [269] ⟨or⟩ would maintain in
Mecklenburg. ⟨So one could … regiment⟩ embark here, land again at Winsen ⟨and⟩ let them go to their quarters or to
the Swedish army. In that way one could also bring the [217] to the [269], if they could not come by Neuenburg ⟨and⟩
through the [231] because of the Leine ⟨and⟩ everything [?…] ⟨While writing I am told …⟩

f. 25, 5 Mar / 23 Feb 1637 read in part

<120> has resolved to content the Reuter; ransom for prisoners; letters of exchange. Block 'work/f025.txt' of reading_segmented.txt.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (93% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

HCP506_reading.md

# HCPortal 506 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, f. 25), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 5 Mar / 23 Feb 1637 — reading of the cipher

Rests on: `f025.txt` (Bourdeau's transcription of scan 0025, one cipher line per scan line) decoded with `key.txt`
unchanged (`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`), the breaks checked on the HCPortal original scan hstam_4_h_1411_0025
(fetched 23 Oct 2026 into the session scratchpad; batch r2b, log `HCP497-506_reread_log.md`). No contemporary
decipherment, clear copy or edition (NOTES, Escalation). Code values: `{Cöln}` = 128, `{die Staaten}` = 188 (grade I,
`codes.txt`).

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[120]` an unvalued code; `<V>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[x]` a letter or word no figure carries; `[…]` unread. Spelling as the cipher gives it (u/v, i/j merged by the
key); nouns capitalised and punctuation added. ⟨...⟩ = clear words. "9 t. Reichst." = 9 thousand Reichsthaler.

## Reading

⟨… stehet zu erwartten,⟩ [120] hatt sich nunmer resolviret, die Furleite zu contentiren; was aber das Furlohn, [R]ancion vor die Gefangene ⟨und⟩ andere Unkosten angehet, darzu will er sich noch nit verstehen, sondern weisset uns auf die embfangene 9 t. Reichst., so [143] auszelen la[?ss]en. <H#> Schreiben an <V> vom <L> 1 Ianua ist nunmer um vierten mal dem [122] zu kommen, ⟨und⟩ pussire ich ihn teglich, das er nach seinem Bruder ⟨und⟩ in den [123] gehen ⟨und⟩ seine Privatsachen i[n] einen Stant richten wol[l]e, damit, wen sich Gelegenheit durch ⟨und⟩ nach {Cöln} zu kommen angebe, er alsdan nit allein schleinig fortgehen ⟨und was auch⟩ ein Zeitlang sich in Hessen aufhalten konte. Wil darauf zwar wohl zu {die Staaten} kommen, aber das er zu [148] verblei[b]en solte, dazu [du]ncket mich habe er wenig Lust zu, ⟨und⟩ wirt <XX> darauf sich wohl nitt zu verlassen haben. ⟨Was schreibet … Und wirdt dort nun nicht⟩ <M> erschienenen Terminen will {Cöln} noch keine Wechselbrieffe bekommen haben. ⟨Thue E. F. G. hiermit in gottes gnädige …⟩

## Notes

- The cipher follows "… stehet zu erwartten," and runs to "… bekommen haben" before the closing formula.
- "hatt": `82 39 A` = h a tt.
- "[R]ancion": `… 72 63 {struck 25} 28? 22 38 11 73 22` = (Furloh)n (struck r) a n c i o n: the writer struck the r of
  "Rancion" (ransom); it is supplied.
- "la[?ss]en": `15 62 D 49 63` = l a ch e n: a ch-sign where ss is needed.
- "<H#> Schreiben an <V> vom <L> 1 Ianua ist": on the scan a crossed H stands alone before "schreiben"; the looped L
  and the stroke after "vom" may be a day of January, not read. "Ianua" = `36 55 44 58 70`, the writer's abbreviation.
- "um vierten mal": checked on the scan (line "31 24 22 56 84 25 …"): the "4#0?" of the transcription is a 40 (z)
  struck through by the writer, and the "12?" before "55 78" is 42 (m): `71 56 88 11 16 30 60 61 44 42 55 78` = u m u
  i e r t e n m a l.
- "pussire" (pousser, to press him) = `81 58 W 36 30 16`.
- "i[n] einen Stant": `95 86 Y 22 16 83 O 39 31 94` = i f ei n e n st a n t: an f-group where n is needed.
- "wol[l]e": `98 73 99 44 79` = w o l n e.
- "sich in Hessen": the figures give "in in" (`11 22 36 44`), the second written twice.
- "verblei[b]en": `47 48 65 91 99 Y 70 49 83` = v e r b l ei a e n: an a-group where b is needed.
- "dazu [du]ncket mich": `27 55 40 71 22 19 51 16 | 60 56 11 N` = d a z u n c k e | t m i ch.
- "<XX>": the doubled sch-sign, as in f. 3 and f. 12, a name sign.
- "<M> erschienenen Terminen": `M 79 25 X 36 16 22 61 44 84 83 26 16 30 | 56 11 63 84 22`; the mm-sign before
  "erschienenen" gives no word ("im" would need an i).
Translation

HCP506_en.md

# HCPortal 506 (f. 25), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 5 Mar / 23 Feb 1637 — English

Translation of `HCP506_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, batch r2b), with the same holes: `[120]` an unvalued code, `<V>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; {Cologne} = 128, {the States} = 188 valued from context.
⟨...⟩ = clear words.

## Translation

⟨… is to be expected,⟩ [120] has now resolved to satisfy the carters; but as for the carriage money, the ransom for the
prisoners ⟨and⟩ other costs, he will not yet agree to that, but refers us to the 9 thousand Reichsthaler received,
which [143] [?has had] paid out. The <H#> letter to <V> of <L> January has now come to the [122] for the fourth time,
⟨and⟩ I press him daily to go after his brother ⟨and⟩ into the [123] ⟨and⟩ put his private affairs in order, so that,
when an opportunity offers to come through ⟨and⟩ to {Cologne}, he may not only leave quickly ⟨and what also⟩ could
stay for a while in Hesse. He is indeed willing to come to {the States}, but to remain at [148] - he has, it seems to
me, little desire for that, ⟨and⟩ <XX> will hardly be able to rely on it. ⟨What … writes … And there now …⟩ at the
terms that have fallen due, {Cologne} claims not yet to have received any bills of exchange. ⟨Commend Your Princely
Grace herewith to God's gracious …⟩

ff. 28-29, 28 Mar 1637 (old style) read in part
