Reading

HCP507_reading.md

# HCPortal 507 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, ff. 28-29), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 28 Mar 1637 (old style) — reading of the cipher

Rests on: `f028.txt` and `f029.txt` (transcription agents, checked by Bourdeau) decoded with `key.txt` unchanged
(`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`), read against the writer's own "Duplicat" of the letter, ff. 30-31 (HCPortal
508, `f030.txt`, `f031.txt`), which was enciphered separately with the same key and line breaks of its own; where the
two copies differ the reading follows the copy whose figures give a word (`HCP507_corrections.tsv`). Breaks looked at
on the HCPortal original scans (hstam_4_h_1411_0028-0031; fetched 22 Oct 2026 into the session scratchpad; batch b08).
No contemporary decipherment, no clear copy, no edition. Code values: `{Compagnien}` = 273, `{Regiment}` = 253,
`{die Staaten}` = 188 (grade I, `codes.txt`).

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b08): decoded, not read; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.437 (before 0.271); holes: open codes and signs, place names outside the word list. Log: `HCP496-509_reread_log.md`. 23 Oct 2026 (batch r2k): read (B) on the German explicit-hole measure, 0.662 (47/71), after a reader's review on the decode and scan 0028 (log `HCP507-509_reread_log.md`); one word re-spelt from the figures (nitt).

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[193]` an unvalued code; `<V>` an unvalued sign (V = the triangle);
`[?word]` partly carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as the cipher gives it: the b08 normalisation (`research/reading_audit/b08_norm.py`) was undone on 23 Oct 2026 (batch r2b, `research/reading_audit/r2b_mals_denorm.py`: each word restored to the form its figures or the transcribed clear words give, e.g. alhir, Feint, schleinig, hette, dan, one); modern spelling only in the English file. punctuation added; the
writer's own parentheses (/: … :/) kept as ( ). Bare figures (6, 1, 2, 3) are written in the cipher as figures.
⟨...⟩ = clear words written in the letter around the cipher.

## Reading

⟨Ich bin gestern abent … wieder glücklich angelangt und habe E. F. G. gnädiges Schreiben … dort mich funden,⟩ wolte mich nun zwar wohl sobalt mit <H#> Train von hier [?aufmachen] ⟨und⟩ zu Land, weil Dunkercker vor etlich Tagen auf den Watten annoch gelegen, durch [235] an bestimmten Ort mit Gottes Hülffe anlangen. Weil ich aber [193] [212] Stück ⟨und⟩ [197] mit bringen so <V>, der Feit aber (welcher noch zwischen [149] ⟨und⟩ Ruhr an die 6 {Compagnien} [193] <e> <n> {Compagnien} [200] ⟨und⟩ 1 {Compagnien} Tragons [?ligen] hat, auch noch <K> {Regiment} [212] auss Lottringen etwa <4#> [?Pferde] starck dazukommen) itzo zusammengehett ⟨und⟩ zu Felt will, so ist uberlant zu gehen nit tunlich; zu Wasser aber langweilig, ⟨und⟩ kan so schleinig nit zu Werck gerichtet werden, damm es erfordert viel Zeit, ehe man Schieffe, so zur See gehen ⟨und⟩ alhier nit zu bekommen sein, bestelt; ⟨und⟩ wohl an die <e> […] {Compagnien} [200] so notwendig von hier abgefurt werden muchen, zwölff {Compagnien} [213] Canons mit gehoriger [208] ⟨und⟩ [XX] Train darauf geladen ⟨und⟩ mit notturfftigen [194] ⟨und⟩ Futterungen versehen werden solten, sein musten, des grossen Unkostens gentzlichen zu geschweigen.

⟨Und⟩ kan contrari Wint kommen, dass man in <f> ⟨oder⟩ <K> Wochen nit einen Schrit weit hinder oder vor sich kommen kan. Zudem so musten die [168] umb einen freien Pass ersucht werden, damm kein Garnison ohn expressen Befehl ein solch Anzal Schieffe würde passiren lassen. Vors dritte würde der Feit [129] Intention darauch leicht abnemen ⟨und⟩ zuvorkommen. Viertens so kan auch über dieses aus disen Quartiren kein [193] (weill di alten {Compagnien} schwach sein ⟨und⟩ die newen nitt auff kommen), wie auch Stücke genommen werden, damm die Commendanten täglich deren beiderlei mehr begeren.

Es sein zwar di <0> newe zwölfpfündige Stücke von [220] alhir, aber noch nitt lavettiret; so hatt man auch keine Kugel ⟨und⟩ [197] Wagen, Constabels ⟨und⟩ Attilage. ⟨Und⟩ i<au> Wartenberg abgedancket, wirt auch nunmer mit [?Frantzosen] sich wider einlassen. Daher auf ihn oder sein {Regiment} ⟨…⟩ unt die Obristen Uffel ⟨und⟩ Karpf aus iren Posten (weill der Feint Feurmorsell ⟨und⟩ Stücke auch m[181] nach Warendorff gefwret, seine Intention aber man annoch nitt [?weiss]) nit zu nemen sein. So begert auch Uffell, weil sin [260] izo so schlecht [?aussehet], nit abzuzancken; auch wirt er ungern in [215] unter[…] stehen [?wollen].

⟨Aber das alles so muss zu fordern und sonderlich⟩ wegen Aussnemung der Völcker ⟨und⟩ wider Bestellung der Commendanten, wen Uffell ⟨und⟩ Carpf [?beide mitgehen] solten, mitt dem <V> alles erst uberlegt ⟨und⟩ abgeredet werden, ⟨welches damm seinen Zuschreiben nach in wenig Tagen alhier sein wird, erfahren⟩ unt Resolution von dem Concluso, wass [126] ⟨und⟩ [153] disen Sommer thun ⟨und⟩ wie balt sie zu Felt gehen werden; 2 ob sie {die Staaten} mitt in ihre Alliance nemen; 3 Musterplatz im Clevischen verstatte, <4#> [XX] bei Lehrort zen Pass ⟨und⟩ Repass über [174] geben ⟨und⟩ darzu eine Brücke herleihen wollen, mitt bringen [?will]. […] doch ohn alles Massgeben (wen es nur dech obigen Feintshalber ist geschehen kan) sich nitt zu übereilen, sondern erst des [122] Widerkunft auss m[214], der dan, sobalt er alhir wider angelangt ist, die [200] nemen ⟨und⟩ zu {die Staaten} gehen will, ⟨und⟩ wohin sich der Feint, welcher beisammn, ⟨weil auch⟩ Jan de Wert so zum Aufbruch sich zwischen Ruhr ⟨und⟩ [268] gefast machet, sich wenden werden, erst zu sehen ⟨und⟩ zu warten hetten.

Dan die Coniuction ⟨…⟩ nirgent eher ⟨und⟩ füglicher als bei Cloppenburg oder Vechte geschehen, damm sie eher von Minden zu Quackenbriegell als zu Verden anderall sein können, ⟨und⟩ haben sie Ossnabruck ander Seiten; kan auch vom Feint ihr kein Schade geschehen, damm Cloppenburg, Vechte, Wiltzhausen und Depholt schlecht besetz sein. Forstenau aber ist mit [oo] <s> […] [193] ⟨und⟩ <o> {Compagnien} [212] belegt; es ist aber die Hase dazwischen, dass sie also von dem Feit, der da in Garnison ist, sicher [?…] [216] wohl gehen ⟨und⟩ dass Reiderlant zuerst, wie der Manssfelter getahn, eingenommen werden kan. Damm die Friessen itzo 1 {Compagnien} [193] von <o>tkopffen werben, auch eine Schantze zwischen Stuckhaussen ⟨und⟩ Bossem an dem Pass, da man ubergehen kan, aufwerffen. […] sollen auch sein die Wege […] so bos, dass man mit Wagen ⟨und⟩ Pferden noch nit darin fortkommen kan.

⟨Habe dar … dieser verantwort,⟩ d[a]ss ich wegen dess Feints Zusammengehen ⟨und⟩ dess [160] Abwesenheit kein [193] [212] ⟨und⟩ Stücke auss den Garnisonen nehmen, auch dass Commendo verendern kan schleinig. ⟨…⟩ Sobalt aber der [122] kombt, soll {die Staaten} auf alle Puncten unterth ⟨unt⟩ aussfurlich beantwortet ⟨und⟩ der <V> sich schleinig durch einen ⟨oder⟩ den andern Weg zu [129] zu erheben pussiret werden. Ob ich aber [?gleichwohl] ⟨und⟩ <+> noch vor s[122] Widerkunft mit {die Staaten} Train allein auf [147] gehen soll, darauf erwarte ich Befehl, ⟨und⟩ will mich auf solchen Fall all Zeit gefast halten.

⟨… stehet bey demselben,⟩ Obrister Hohendorf ist ⟨unterweges bey mich⟩ kommen, berichtet, dass er den Rest von seinem [191] etwa <4H> man widerholten in den Fegesack geschicket hette, welcher vermeinte, mit seinen Völckern nunmehr dass Land vom Hadelen, so Sachsen-Lauenburg zustendig ist, einzunemen ⟨und⟩ daselbst Quartier zu suchen; ist auch nach m[123] zu [?Rantzo] gezogen.

## Notes

- Word division, i/j and u/v as the sense needs; "Feit" (= Feind, l. 12, 'ferEIt' in both copies), "dach" (= dass),
  "seher" (= eher), "zen" (= den), "dech" (= des), "darauch" (= daraus) are the writer's slips as the figures give them,
  kept.
- "ligen": f. 28 `99 Π? 54 79 83` (a gate-like sign), f. 30 `limen`; partly carried.
- "Pferde": f. 28 `pferi`, f. 30 `pnert`; "Pferd(e) starck".
- "bestimten": both copies write `be 7 imten`, the lone 7 standing for st.
- "wohl an di <e> […]": f. 28 `wohlandii [e] wen tt`, f. 30 `… [e] len tt`; no word comes out.
- "wider Bestellung": f. 28 has `[128] iderbeSTeLLeng`; f. 30 has `widerbeSTeLLung`, so the "128" of f. 28 is a w-group
  and is not read as the code for Cöln (corrections file).
- "i<au> Wartenberg abgedancket": `i S` in both copies; "ist" would need a t; open.
- "Frantzosen": `miTT rantzosiCH` in both copies; the F is not written and the ending is "sich"; partly carried.
- "weiss": `w EICH` (f. 30), struck 44 in f. 28; "aussehet": `AU e het`; "wollen": f. 28 `wo SCH en`, f. 30 `wo [U?] en`.
- "beide mitgehen": `bEI de mi LL gehen` in both copies; the ll-sign stands where t is needed.
- "will": f. 28 `wi SCH`, f. 30 `wi [U?]`, followed by clear words read differently in the two transcriptions.
- "gleichwohl": `m lEICH uohl` in both copies, m for g.
- "Rantzo": f. 29 `zu radt zo gezogen`, f. 31 `zu rantz o gezogen`.
- "Hohendorf", "Rest", "Forstenau aber" follow f. 31/f. 30 where f. 29 has `hohtndorf`, `rz st`, `AU waeer`.
- Place names: Lehrort (Lerort, the Rhine-Lippe ford by Wesel), Quakenbrück, Verden, Osnabrück, Cloppenburg, Vechta,
  Wildeshausen, Diepholz, Fürstenau, the Hase, the Reiderland, Stickhausen, Vegesack, Hadeln (Saxe-Lauenburg).
Translation

HCP507_en.md

# HCPortal 507 (ff. 28-29, with the duplicate 508, ff. 30-31), Malsburg to Landgrave Wilhelm V, 28 Mar 1637 (old style) — English

Translation of `HCP507_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b08), with the same holes: `[193]` an unvalued code, `<V>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; {companies} = 273, {regiment} = 253, {the States} = 188 valued
from context. ⟨...⟩ = clear words. Partial reading: the document stays "decoded, not read".

## Translation

⟨I arrived back safely yesterday evening … and found Your Princely Grace's gracious letter … there,⟩ I would indeed like to set out from here with <H#> train as soon as possible ⟨and⟩ by land, since Dunkirkers were lying on the Watten a few days ago, and arrive by [235] at the appointed place with God's help. But since I am to bring [193] [212] guns ⟨and⟩ [197] <V>, and the enemy (who, between [149] ⟨and⟩ the Ruhr, has about 6 {companies} [193] <e> <n> {companies} [200] ⟨and⟩ 1 {company} of dragoons [?lying], and besides <K> {regiment} [212] from Lorraine, about <4#> [?horse] strong, coming to join) now gathers ⟨and⟩ wants to take the field, it is not advisable to go overland; by water, though, it is slow ⟨and⟩ cannot be put in hand so quickly, for it takes a great deal of time before ships that go to sea ⟨and⟩ are not to be had here are ordered; ⟨and⟩ […] {companies} [200] have to be taken away from here so necessarily, twelve {companies} [213] cannons, with proper [208] ⟨and⟩ [XX] train loaded on them ⟨and⟩ provided with the necessary [194] ⟨and⟩ fodder, would have to be, to say nothing of the great cost.

⟨And⟩ a contrary wind can come, so that in <f> ⟨or⟩ <K> weeks one cannot move a step backward or forward. Moreover, the [168] would have to be asked for a free passage, for no garrison would let such a number of ships pass without express orders. Thirdly, the enemy would easily guess [129]'s intention from it ⟨and⟩ forestall it. Fourthly, besides this, no [193] can be taken from these quarters (because the old {companies} are weak ⟨and⟩ the new ones are not coming up), nor guns either, since the commanders daily demand more of both.

There are indeed the <0> new twelve-pounder guns from [220] here, but not yet mounted on carriages; nor does one have any shot ⟨or⟩ [197] wagons, gunners ⟨and⟩ equipment. ⟨And⟩ Wartenberg [has been] discharged, and will now take service again with the [?French]. Therefore it is not possible to take him or his {regiment} ⟨…⟩ and the colonels Uffel ⟨and⟩ Karpf from their posts (because the enemy has brought fire-mortars ⟨and⟩ guns m[181] to Warendorf, but one does not yet [?know] his intention). Uffel also asks, since his [260] now looks so bad, not to be wrangled with; and he will not willingly [?want] to stand under […] in [215].

⟨But all this must be demanded, and especially⟩ about the withdrawal of the troops ⟨and⟩ the re-appointment of commanders, if Uffel ⟨and⟩ Karpf were [?both to go along], everything must first be considered ⟨and⟩ agreed with the <V>, ⟨which, according to his letter, will be here in a few days, to learn⟩ and the resolution of the conclusum, what [126] ⟨and⟩ [153] will do this summer ⟨and⟩ how soon they will take the field; 2, whether they will take {the States} into their alliance; 3, whether they will allow a muster place in the Cleves country, <4#> [XX] give passage ⟨and⟩ repassage at Lehrort over [174] ⟨and⟩ lend a bridge for it, [?will] bring along. […] yet, without any presumption (if it can only be done on account of the enemy above), not to rush, but first to wait for the return of [122] from m[214], who, as soon as he has arrived here again, wants to take the [200] ⟨and⟩ go to {the States}, ⟨and⟩ to see first where the enemy, who is together, will turn ⟨since⟩ Jan de Werth is also getting ready to march between the Ruhr ⟨and⟩ [268].

For the conjunction ⟨…⟩ could happen nowhere sooner ⟨and⟩ more conveniently than at Cloppenburg or Vechta, since they could be sooner from Minden to Quakenbrück than to Verden or elsewhere, ⟨and⟩ they have Osnabrück on the other side; nor can the enemy do them any harm, since Cloppenburg, Vechta, Wildeshausen and Diepholz are poorly garrisoned. Fürstenau, however, is occupied with [oo] <s> […] [193] ⟨and⟩ <o> {companies} [212]; but the Hase lies between, so that they can go safely [?…] [216] from the enemy who is in garrison there, ⟨and⟩ that the Reiderland can be taken first, as the Mansfelder did. For the Frisians are now recruiting 1 {company} [193] from <o>tkopffen, and are throwing up a sconce between Stickhausen ⟨and⟩ Bossem at the pass where one can cross. […] the roads are also said to be […] so bad that one cannot yet get through them with wagons ⟨and⟩ horses.

⟨I have … answer for this,⟩ that, because of the enemy's gathering ⟨and⟩ the absence of [160], I cannot take any [193] [212] ⟨or⟩ guns out of the garrisons, nor change the command quickly. ⟨…⟩ But as soon as [122] comes, {the States} shall be answered on all points humbly ⟨and⟩ in detail, ⟨and⟩ the <V> urged to set out quickly by one ⟨or⟩ the other road to [129]. Whether I [?nevertheless] ⟨and⟩ <+> should go to [147] alone with {the States}' train even before the return of [122], on that I await orders, ⟨and⟩ will keep myself ready for that case at all times.

⟨… stands with him,⟩ Colonel Hohendorf came ⟨to me on the way⟩ and reports that he had sent the rest of his [191], some <4H> men, back to the Vegesack, who intended now to take the Land of Hadeln, which belongs to Saxe-Lauenburg, with his troops ⟨and⟩ to seek quarters there; he has also gone to m[123] at [?Rantzau].

## Notes

- Lehrort: the Rhine crossing below Wesel. Mansfelder: Count Ernst von Mansfeld, who occupied East Frisia in 1622-1624.

ff. 30-31, 'Duplicat' of ff. 28-29 decoded, not read

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

ff. 32-33, 3 Apr 1637 and Mar 1637 decoded, not read

A French letter signed '232' of 3 Apr 1637, citing a letter of the ambassador extraordinary in London, and German news extracts from London of 16/26 Mar in a second hand, in the same key; paired large capitals are name signs. Blocks 'work/f032.txt' and 'work/f033.txt' of reading_segmented.txt.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Reading

HCP509_reading.md

# HCPortal 509 (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, ff. 32-33): the French letter of "232", 3 Apr 1637, and German news extracts — partial reading

Rests on: `f032.txt`, `f033.txt` (transcription agents, checked by Bourdeau) decoded with `key.txt` unchanged
(`research/reading_audit/b08_dec.py`); the clear French letter on f. 32 transcribed here from the HCPortal original scan
(hstam_4_h_1411_0032, 2114 x 2512, fetched 22 Oct 2026 into the session scratchpad; batch b08) and the cipher runs set
in it. The German extracts (lower half of f. 32 and f. 33, a second, fast hand) are given only as far as the 28 Sept
transcription carries their clear words; their clear text was not re-transcribed in this pass. No contemporary
decipherment, no clear copy, no edition. One label corrected on the scan (`HCP509_corrections.tsv`,
`f032_corrected.txt`). Code values: `{die Staaten}` = 188 (grade I, `codes.txt`).

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b08): decoded, not read (French letter read; German extracts not). 23 Oct 2026 (batch r2k): the German extracts' clear text (second hand, lower f. 32 and f. 33, three pieces) transcribed line by line on 2x crops of the HCPortal scans 0032-0033 and set around the decoded cipher runs; see the Notes. Still decoded, not read: explicit holes 0.667 overall, but the German extracts alone 0.50 (log `HCP507-509_reread_log.md`). Log `HCP496-509_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a context value; `[129]` an unvalued code; `<GG>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread or not transcribed. Cipher words are in *italics*; everything else is clear
text in the letter. Spelling as written; punctuation added.

## Reading

Monseigneur, combien que la retraicte de Gallas de France, comme aussy l'accord porté par le Roy Treschrestien au mariage de Monsieur et le retour de Madame sa femme, qu'on croid qui s'ensuivra bientost, et le passage de nouveau ouvert pour les lettres et correspondences par la [?Brabande], donnent quelque soupçon a quelques uns qu'il y pourroit estre bonne correspondence entre les Couronnes France et Espagne, si est ce que je suis a present d'un contraire advis, et espere que tout ira mieulx qu'on ne s'est proposé n'agueres, estant confirmé grandement en mon opinion par la lettre *de nre Ambassadeur extraordinaire qui est a Londres, de la […] [?veu] […] la copie*. Il me faict l'honneur de tenir estroicte correspondence avec moy; aussy j'ay addressé toutes mes lettres *a Mons Sengel a Londres*, laissant tousjours mes [lettres] que je [?sers] *audict Ambassadeur* a cachet volant, afin que *ledict Sengell* soit informé entierement de ce qui passe icy. Il est fort content que je l'ay [?…] addressé *audict [?Ambassadeur]*, et honore sa personne selon que ses dignes qualitez le meritent aussy. Une chose me desplaist neantmoins, c'est a scavoir que [?Anger] tarde tant en France. Car puis qu'il est asseuré qu'il porte plus de contentement de Angleterre au Roy Treschrestien que le Roy mesme n'avoit demandé, je trouve estrange qu'on le traisne tant a Paris. Peut estre que c'est a quelque bonne fin. Mes lettres du 27e de Mars de Paris disent que six armées du Roy marcheront dans peu de jours, dont quatre sont composées une chascune de dix mille hommes et les deux aultres une chascune de douze mille hommes, tellement que la guerre y commence a bons escient. J'espere aussy que Vre Altze par la desmarche desdittes armées sera bien soulagée, et j'espere aussy qu'on faira de ce costé icy une bonne diversion. Et n'ayant aultre chose pour le present, je remettray les nouvelles que nous attendons de jour a aultre du traicté conclu entre les deux couronnes au prochain messager, me signant tousjours, Hault et Puissant Seigneur, Monseigneur, de Vre Altze treshumble, tresobeissant et tres fidel serviteur, 232.

Extract *[?Ambassadeur]* schreiben aus *[115] aus [?London]* vom 16/26 Mart. 1637.

In Franckreich ist man nicht wohl zufrieden, dass der Hertzog von Parma, wie man [?nicht] vermeinet, gehabt eine neutralitet gehandelt zu haben, sich auch verpflichtet [?Saindetta], den besten importanten Platz, dem Hertzog von Medina, Viceroy von [?Sicilien], in handen zu stellen, Und dass in Piacenza Und Parma [?Hochdeutsche] Und Italianer unter [?] mit [?einigen] officirern in guarnison gelegt werden sollen. Herr [?] Landtgraff [?] das [?wesen] thut wohl seine sache in acht zu nehmen Und Franckreich, dass er ihm darin die hand [?bieten]; [?verspricht] auch dass demselben [?widerumb] eine gute [...]. Es ist ein Werck das [?gar] viel meritiret, Und da er nit will, solten [?ihr] [?selbst] das encouragiren, so viel [?bey] der [?Zustandt] leiden will; ob ist allzeit besser etwas gethan Und dadurch seine gewogenheit zu bezeugen, als nichts. *[248]* solte billich das *meinste thun*, weil *[129] der Pfalz der nechst*, Und also durch denselben [?directe] [?] [?oder] etwas solte ihm können, dahin das *<OO>* am meisten geneigt. Ob ist aber vor erst das hier *nicht viel sec<K>rs zu hoffen* als *[…] zu wasser unterm beleit vom [155] selbst*, gleich wohl helt *[148] Sengel nuff* bis sicherheit vom schluss mit Franckreich kömbt. Immittelst überlegt man wie *{die Staaten}* Und *<AA>* contentement zu geben, Und wohin *Printz Robert* [?] [?], ob [?sich] [?] *<XX>* oder *<▢>* nach *<GG>* scheinet abzuführen; doch ist gut zu apparent, das *[155]* mit hülffe *<CC>* soll ahnnehmen *[129] armee zu unterhal[t]en*, Und damit bringt [?ex] [?] nach *Palz* zu thun. Da das [?Al]liance [?ist] geschlossen, wirdt *[133] entlich brechen* müssen; *[155]* solle vermög derselben [?sonsten] *im Apr mit* Und *Schiff z [126] stoch* Und das demselben seine Commission [?empfangen].

Da aber gleich die alliance nicht geschlossen wirdt, hat gleichwohl *der Churf mit denen [?eiffen] zu disponirn sich* mit *<GG> <AA>* oder *<KK>*. In Engellandt gehet man damit umb eine Comp. nach Ost Indien [?aufzurichten], dann der König die gerechtigkeit der Admiralitet Und acten seinem Siegel zu geben [?versprochen], wie auch die [?bey] [?ihren] Congnesten zu maintenieren; Metter tijt compt Herman met wambas en de broeck, Staet te hopen dat metter tijt ock sal volgen; das Pfältzische manifest so ziemblich weitleuffig, wirdt balt heraus kommen. *<V> aus [123]*, 5 April / 26 Mart. 1637.

Sonsten stehen die handlungen mit Schweden, Franckreich Und Engellandt *noch sehr dunkel*; gehen *selzame dinge umb* mit *<LL>*; habe ich auch [?beweglichst] anhalten [?sache] [?gewust], *ist alles umsonst*. Hat der Pass durch [?] [?offen], [?] sagt man [?frey] [?dass] der Kauffleut zu [?Andorff] [?geschehen]; ob wirdt berichtet, dass la Valette mit einem Heer die Mosell herab kommt Und 20000 man in Italien geschickt werden. Es hat es mit dem bündt zwischen Franckreich Und Engellandt [?schwer] [?zu] [?haben] sein, darumb weil Franckreich will, dass man [?Schweden] Und Hollandt mit lassen begreiffen solle; Schweden will itzo von St. Chaumond etliche erklerung wissen, ob Franckreich offenbar feindt sein wolle mit dem Kayser; Franckreich will den König in Ungarn nicht kennen als Königl. König. *Hoff vo[n] [156]*, 17/27 Martij 1637.

Zu Paris hat man mit abfertigung Hertzog Bernhards von Sachsen Und la Valette so viel zu thun, dass andere verrichtungen Und expeditiones verschoben werden. Der Hertzog von Rohan hat eine gute victori wider die Spanier erhalten, so haben die Frantzosen [?] [?Landtgrund] Und [?] 1200 man erlegt Und 200 gefangen, [?dessen] die relation des Curirers [?gemeldet]; der Obrist Roß hat vor wenig tagen ein Gallasisch Regiment geschlagen.

## Notes

- French letter: the clear text is transcribed here from the scan; the 28 Sept transcription gave only the words next
  to the cipher. [?Brabande] (perhaps "la Brabande"/"le Brabant"), [?Anger] (a person's name, "Anger" or "Augier") and
  the word before "addressé" ("bydomme") are uncertain in the clear hand.
- Cipher runs of the French letter: `46 79 31 97 16 | 39 42 76 39 W 28 46 [struck] 97 48 20 60 65 87 35 30 67 11 63 Q
  97 49 34 71 66 48 Z 39 99 [struck] 31 46 25 16 68` = de nre ambassad[e]r extraordinaire qui est a l[o]ndres (Q = ai on the
  scan; the transcription's "MM" is a misread, corrections file); `67 79 15 62 17 88 84 [struck] 76 79 58 93 38 80 14
  99 70 19 93 81 36 79` = de la cue … beu occu la copie: "de la[quelle]" and "veu" (b for v) are guesses, so the clause
  stays holed; `39 42 73 31 68 77 84 83 29 49 99 55 15 73 22 32 30 84 13` = a mons sengel a londres; `39 R 91 55 W 62
  27 Y 30` = ambassadeir; `78 49 27 36 38 26 68 61 22 43 16 H` = ledict sengell; `S 27 36 38 94 Z 76 70 39 67 79 71 25`
  = audict st ba adeur (the ambassador again, with struck groups).
- "Sengel": the Hessian correspondent in London; "232" signs the letter (sender not identified).
- German extracts (23 Oct 2026, r2k): the clear text of the second hand transcribed on the scan (crops of hstam_4_h_1411_0032
  y 1420-2500 and 0033 y 220-1500 at 2-3x, session scratchpad reread/r2k/); the fast Kurrent hand leaves single words
  uncertain ([?word]) or unread ([?]); the decoded cipher runs (italics) are unchanged from b08. Spelling as written; the
  Dutch proverb on f. 33 kept as written. 'Saindetta' is probably Sabbioneta, 'Congnesten' conquests, 'Roß' Colonel
  Rosen; 'Andorff' Antwerp. 'secKrs' = secours (K sign), 'Palz' = Pfalz as the figures give it.
Translation

HCP509_en.md

# HCPortal 509 (ff. 32-33): the French letter of "232", 3 Apr 1637, and German news extracts — English

Translation of `HCP509_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b08; German extracts re-transcribed 23 Oct 2026, batch r2k), with the same holes: `[129]` an unvalued code, `<GG>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread or not transcribed; cipher in *italics*;
{the States} = 188 valued from context. Partial reading: the document stays "decoded, not read".

## Translation

My lord, although the retreat of Gallas from France, as also the agreement brought by the Most Christian King to the marriage of Monsieur and the return of Madame his wife, which it is believed will follow soon, and the passage newly opened for letters and correspondence through the [?Brabant], give some people some suspicion that there might be good understanding between the Crowns of France and Spain, yet I am now of a contrary opinion, and hope that all will go better than was proposed not long ago, being greatly confirmed in my opinion by the letter *of our ambassador extraordinary who is in London, of the […] [?seen] […] the copy*. He does me the honour of keeping close correspondence with me; I have also addressed all my letters *to Monsieur Sengel in London*, always leaving my [letters] that I [?send] *to the said ambassador* under flying seal, so that *the said Sengel* may be fully informed of what passes here. He is very content that I have [?…] addressed it *to the said [?ambassador]*, and honours his person as his worthy qualities also deserve. One thing displeases me, however, namely that [?Anger] is so long delayed in France. For since it is certain that he brings more satisfaction from England to the Most Christian King than the King himself had asked, I find it strange that he is kept dragging on so long in Paris. Perhaps it is for some good end. My letters of 27 March from Paris say that six armies of the King will march in a few days, four of them of ten thousand men each and the other two of twelve thousand men each, so that the war there is beginning in earnest. I also hope that Your Highness will be much relieved by the march of the said armies, and I also hope that a good diversion will be made on this side. And having nothing else for the present, I shall leave the news we expect from day to day of the treaty concluded between the two crowns to the next messenger, signing myself always, High and Mighty Lord, my lord, Your Highness's most humble, most obedient and most faithful servant, 232.

Extract of the *[?ambassador's]* letter from *[115] from [?London]* of 16/26 March 1637.

In France they are not well pleased that the Duke of Parma, as one [?did not] expect, is said to have negotiated a neutrality and also bound himself to put [?Sabbioneta], the best and most important place, into the hands of the Duke of Medina, Viceroy of [?Sicily], and that in Piacenza and Parma [?High Germans] and Italians under [?] with [?some] officers are to be put in garrison. Lord [?] Landgrave [?] the [?affair] does well to look to his business, and France [hopes] that he [?will offer] him his hand in it; [?it promises] also that [?in return] a good [...] to him. It is a work that deserves [?much], and if he will not, [?they] [?themselves] should encourage it as far as the [?situation] will allow; for it is always better to have done something and thereby shown one's goodwill than nothing. *[248]* should rightly *do the most*, because *[129] of the Palatinate the next*, and so through him [?directly] [?] [?or] something should be able to [do] for him, to which the *<OO>* is most inclined. But for the present there is *not much succour to hope for* here other than *[…] by water under the escort of [155] himself*; nevertheless *[148] Sengel* holds [back] until security comes from the conclusion with France. Meanwhile they consider how to give *{the States}* and *<AA>* satisfaction, and where *Prince Rupert* [?] [?], whether [?] *<XX>* or *<▢>* seems to lead towards *<GG>*; yet it is quite apparent that *[155]* with the help of *<CC>* is to undertake *to maintain [129] army*, and so bring [?] to do towards the *Palatinate*. When the alliance [?is] concluded, *[133] will finally have to break*; *[155]* is by virtue of it [?otherwise] *in April with*, and *ship z [126] stoch*, and that he has [?received] his commission.

But even if the alliance is not concluded, *the Elector* has nevertheless *to dispose himself with those [?]* with *<GG> <AA>* or *<KK>*. In England they are about [?setting up] a Company for the East Indies, for the King has [?promised] to give the right of the Admiralty and the acts under his seal, as also to maintain [?their] conquests; "In time comes Herman with doublet and breeches; it is to be hoped that in time it will follow too" (Dutch proverb); the Palatine manifesto, fairly long, will soon come out. *<V> from [123]*, 5 April / 26 March 1637.

Otherwise the negotiations with Sweden, France and England stand *still very dark*; *strange things are going on* with *<LL>*; I have also [?urgently] pressed [?] [?], *it is all in vain*. Whether the pass through [?] [?is open], [?] they say [?freely] [?that] of the merchants at [?Antwerp] [?happened]; it is reported that La Valette is coming down the Moselle with an army and that 20,000 men are to be sent into Italy. The league between France and England [?is hard to obtain], because France wants [?Sweden] and Holland to be included in it; Sweden now wants a declaration from Saint-Chaumont whether France will be the Emperor's open enemy; France will not recognise the King of Hungary as King [of the Romans]. *Court of [156]*, 17/27 March 1637.

In Paris they have so much to do with the dispatch of Duke Bernhard of Saxony and La Valette that other business and dispatches are put off. The Duke of Rohan has won a good victory against the Spaniards; the French have [?] [?] and [?] killed 1,200 men and taken 200 prisoner, [?as] the courier's report [?says]; Colonel Rosen a few days ago beat a regiment of Gallas.

06 Sources

  • Hessisches Staatsarchiv Marburg, HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, ff. 3–33; scans via HCPortal, cryptograms 496, 497 and 502–509.
  • HCPortal cipher keys of HStAM 4 d Nr. 1218–1236 (176 records), in particular key 5, “Clavis … Sixtinus … im Decemb. anno 1635”, and key 31 (name codes).
  • E. Antal and P. Mírka, “Wrong design of cipher keys: analysis of historical cipher keys from the Hessisches Staatsarchiv Marburg used in the Thirty Years’ War”, HistoCrypt 2022.
  • The alphabetic block: authentic image 0012 (2834 × 4284 pixels), HCPortal 497, HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411. Only source images and ordinary crops were used, without generated detail.
  • Larry Beck with ChatGPT, alphabetic critical edition and replay evidence, 2 October 2026. This contribution preserves Bourdeau’s inherited transcription alongside the revised source branch and identifies AI-assisted readings as such.
  • Franz von Geyso, Beiträge zur Politik und Kriegführung Hessens, part III, ZHG 55 (1926), p. 99 n. 2 (16 November 1636 letter), pp. 115-117 (January retention offers). Secondary paraphrases; the cited manuscript letters remain uninspected.
  • Wilhelm Hofmann, Peter Melander, Reichsgraf zu Holzappel (1882), pp. 68-69. Geyso warns that the printed quotation differs from the original; it is not an independently collated January letter.
  • HStAM 4 d Nr. 1218 Teil 1, Arcinsys description 1923393, images 0040-0042 (key 5), 0121-0122 (key 30), 0123-0126 (key 31). Comparison gives no independently matched January code value.
  • Documentary retrieval leads, catalogue records only: LHA Koblenz 47/4400 (cipher codes, 1635-1641); HStAM 4 h 2208 (July-December 1636, not a proved concordance for Kr. A. 1636, III); HStAM 4 h 2209 (January-March 1637); and HStAM 4 f Staaten F, Frankreich 1297 (Hoff reports, 1636-1637).
